# Raiju vs Bifrost: feature parity audit

Re-audited 2026-10-14 against the code at d27a62e; G2 (auth_config flags) and the retries metric were fixed right after.

Date: 2026-10-14. Target: Bifrost `main` at commit `6c84d7a` (shallow clone in `/tmp/claude-0/bifrost-src`). Raiju: read at commit `2923fea` plus the uncommitted working-tree edits present at read time (`src/logstore.tov`, `src/metrics.tov`, `src/ops.tov`, `src/semcache.tov`, `src/server.tov`; `/metrics` calls `ops.metricsText()`). Those edits were committed during the audit as `b506bfc`, and HEAD is now `32c5de8` (Tov patches only). The working tree matches HEAD, so the line numbers below hold for HEAD. This audit changed nothing else in the repo and made no commits.

Bifrost paths are relative to the Bifrost repo root. Raiju paths are relative to `/home/user/raiju`. Line numbers are given where they were read.

## Method

Re-audit (2026-10-14): every partial and missing row was checked against `src/` at d27a62e. Each row's Bifrost route, header or key was grepped in the code, and the commit that claims it was read. Done rows were spot-checked, not all re-read. Counts were computed from the table rows by script. The Summary's route coverage figures are from the original audit and were not recomputed.

- Bifrost routes: every `r.GET/POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE` registration in `transports/bifrost-http/handlers`, plus the per-SDK route configs in `transports/bifrost-http/integrations/*.go`, mounted by `handlers/integrations.go:33-51`. Cross-checked against `docs/openapi/openapi.json` (511 paths, 669 method-path operations).
- Bifrost config: `transports/config.schema.json` (top-level properties and `$defs`).
- Bifrost headers: string literals in `transports/bifrost-http/lib/ctx.go` (request) and `lib/responseheaders.go` (response), plus the header reference in `docs/`.
- Bifrost providers and per-operation support: `core/schemas/bifrost.go` (provider and request-type constants) and the "Supported Operations" tables in `docs/providers/supported-providers/*.mdx`.
- Bifrost plugins and metrics: `plugins/*/main.go`, `plugins/telemetry/main.go` (metric names), `core/schemas/enrichment.go` (metric label dimensions).
- Raiju: `src/server.tov` dispatch, `src/media.tov`, `src/native.tov`, `src/extras.tov`, `src/jobs.tov`, `src/store.tov`, `src/ops.tov`, `src/governance.tov`, `src/gateway.tov`, `src/config.tov`, plus `README.md`, `SCOPE.md`, `LEDGER.md`. Routes, headers and config keys marked missing were checked by grep against `src/` and `ui/`.
- Route coverage (`/tmp/claude-0/audit/coverage.py`): Raiju's registered method and path shapes were matched against the documented OpenAPI operations. This measures path shape only. It does not check that a matched route behaves the same.

Status values:

- **done**: works as Bifrost documents it, for the cases checked.
- **partial**: exists, but a documented option, provider, operation or field is missing or behaves differently. The notes say which.
- **missing**: no route, header, key or code path.
- **out-of-scope**: listed in `SCOPE.md` under "Out".
- **n/a**: Bifrost itself removed the feature, or it is not GA upstream, or it is a Go-runtime detail with no HTTP or config meaning. Raiju is not penalised for these.
- Rows marked "Raiju-only" have no Bifrost counterpart. They are listed so the reader can see what Raiju adds.

Priority: P0 means a Bifrost user would hit it on the first day of switching, or it is a silent security or budget failure. P1 means a common Bifrost workflow breaks or behaves differently. P2 means a long-tail feature, a feature outside `SCOPE.md`, or a decision still to be made.

<!-- parity-count:end -->

The `n/a` rows are features Bifrost has removed (x-bf-azure-endpoint, x-bf-bedrock-*, x-bf-prom-*), features Bifrost has not shipped (Google and GitHub SSO), a managed offering (InVPC), or Go-runtime settings with no HTTP or config meaning. The `out-of-scope` rows are the SCOPE.md "Out" items: videos, containers, media providers (Runware included), Warp, the skills marketplace and Sidekiq (added in L96d).

Open items after the L97 waves are listed in the Remaining gaps section at the end.

Route coverage by path shape:

| Set | Documented operations | Shape-matched in Raiju |
|---|---|---|
| All Bifrost OpenAPI operations | 669 | 85 (13%) |
| Inference and SDK route families, excluding videos and containers | 223 | 45 (20%) |
| Management and admin (`/api/*`, agents, OAuth) | 412 | 38 (9%) |

At the 2026-10-14 audit, several `SCOPE.md` entries overstated parity. "OpenAI SDK route", "Bedrock SDK route", "Google GenAI SDK route", "Anthropic SDK route", "Audio", "Images", "Files and batches", "Realtime", "config.json compatibility" and "Prometheus metrics" were marked "done", but each had partial or missing pieces listed below. Gap G32 tracked this; SCOPE.md was brought in line with these rows in L96d.

## (a) HTTP routes

Re-audited 2026-10-15 against HEAD (3183cf4) and Bifrost `6c84d7a`. Raiju notes name the file and function, not line numbers. L101 and L102 are merged; their rows are updated in this file (L96d).

### a1. Native /v1 API

Bifrost: `transports/bifrost-http/handlers/inference.go`, `asyncinference.go`, `mcpinference.go`, `wsrealtime.go`, `webrtcrealtime.go`, `realtime_client_secrets.go`, `wsresponses.go`, `livecontrol.go`, `wslive.go`. Documented in `docs/openapi/openapi.json`.

| Feature | Bifrost | Raiju status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| POST /v1/chat/completions | handlers/inference.go; openapi | done | `server.tov` `dispatch` (CHAT_PATHS); streamed by `server.tov` `chatStreamed` (Bun) and `front.tov` (http1). |
| POST /v1/completions | handlers/inference.go | done | `server.tov` `dispatch`. Text completions for OpenAI-format providers (`gateway.tov` `streamsNatively`). L163: Anthropic sends Anthropic's legacy `/v1/complete` (`providers/anthropic.tov` textRequest, textResponse: the prompt framed in "Human:/Assistant:" turns as Bifrost frames it, max_tokens_to_sample 4096 when unset) and Bedrock InvokeModel with Claude's legacy body or Mistral's plain body (`providers/bedrock.tov` textRequest, textResponse; another Bedrock model is refused with Bifrost's "unsupported model type for text completion"), wired in `gateway.tov` completions. A streamed request to Anthropic is refused, as Bifrost has no Anthropic text stream. Other providers answer "does not serve text completions", as Bifrost does for Gemini, Cohere and Vertex without its compat text-to-chat plugin (checked in Bifrost `core/bifrost.go`: the chat conversion needs `BifrostContextKeyChangeRequestType`, which only the compat plugin sets). L173: Bedrock's streamed text completion goes to InvokeModelWithResponseStream with the same InvokeModel body, as Bifrost's TextCompletionStream (`providers/bedrock.tov` textStreamRequest); its "chunk" events carry the model's own JSON in base64 bytes (Claude's completion and stop_reason, Mistral's outputs), read as they arrive and sent as text_completion chunks, the usage from the last chunk's invocation metrics (`streams.tov` BedrockEventStreamToOpenAI invokeText, `gateway.tov` completions). Bifrost sends each chunk as an empty text completion that carries the model's JSON only as its raw response; Raiju sends the text. A stream that cannot be piped is asked whole and answered as one text_completion frame (`gateway.tov` textAnswerSse). |
| POST /v1/responses (create, stream) | handlers/inference.go; supported-providers/*.mdx | done | `server.tov` `dispatch` → `native.tov` `responsesApi`. Native for OpenAI, translated for other chat providers. |

| POST /v1/responses/input_tokens | handlers/inference.go | done | `media.tov` `PATTERNS` → `handleMedia`. |

| POST /v1/embeddings | handlers/inference.go | done | `server.tov` `dispatch` → `gateway.tov` `Engine.embeddings`. |
| POST /v1/rerank | handlers/inference.go | done | `extras.tov` `rerank` and `rerankText`. Cohere, vLLM and Bedrock answer natively; model `raiju/lexical` asks for the local BM25 ranking. Other providers return an error. |

Vertex, Bedrock and Anthropic answer 400: Bifrost has no speech for them either (vertex.mdx, bedrock.mdx). |

OpenAI-compatible and Azure pass through. Gemini (`media_native.tov` `geminiImageEdit`: the images inline on generateContent, or Imagen's :predict with reference images, mask and edit mode), Vertex (`media_cloud.tov` `vertexImageEdit`), Bedrock (`media_cloud.tov` `bedrockImageEdit`: inpainting, outpainting, background removal). L97q: Bedrock Stability AI edits too (`media_cloud.tov` `stabilityImageEdit`, `stabilityEditBody`: the task from the form's type or the model id, Bifrost's per-task options; the options ride in the form as `media.tov` `formExtras`). L174: the form's other text fields (every field Bifrost's edit route does not read itself, its imageEditParamsKnownFields, and a named option the task does not take) go into the Stability body as the strings the form sent, merged as Bifrost merges ExtraParams, which its edit route always passes on (a key the body has is replaced, new keys last in sorted order; `media.tov` formExtras, `media_cloud.tov` editKnownField, stabilityEditBody); e2e `l174_e2e`. L194: Hugging Face edits on fal-ai (data URLs in image_url or image_urls, the generation options, `stream` on fal-ai's /stream route; `media.tov` hfMedia) and Replicate's edit options mapped as Bifrost maps them (see Images in (d2)). Open (owner decision 6): Stability variations, which Bifrost also does not shape (it sends the Titan body; Raiju refuses them). |

Gemini and Vertex answer 400, as Bifrost has no variation for them. |

Cohere has no files API upstream (Bifrost's Cohere page lists Files as unsupported). |
| GET/DELETE /v1/files/{id}, GET /v1/files/{id}/content | handlers/inference.go | done | `media.tov` `PATTERNS` → `handleMedia`. Same providers as the file list. |
| POST /v1/batches, GET /v1/batches | handlers/inference.go | done | `media.tov` `PATTERNS` → `handleMedia`; native for Anthropic (`anthropic_batch.tov`), Gemini, Vertex (`media_cloud.tov` batch jobs) and Bedrock (`media_cloud.tov` S3 jobs). Cohere has no batch API upstream. |
| GET /v1/batches/{id}, POST /v1/batches/{id}/cancel | handlers/inference.go | done | `media.tov` `PATTERNS` → `handleMedia`. Same providers as the batch list. |
| GET /v1/batches/{id}/results | handlers/inference.go | done | `media.tov` `PATTERNS` → `handleMedia` (batches.results in each provider branch). Anthropic and Gemini output is also readable through `/v1/files/{id}/content`. |
| GET /v1/models | handlers/inference.go | done | `server.tov` `listModels`. Upstream listing for OpenAI-format, Anthropic and Gemini. |
| GET /v1/models/{model} | handlers/inference.go (modelRetrieve) | done | `server.tov` `retrieveModel` → `model_retrieve.tov` `retrieveRequest` and `retrieveBody`. OpenAI-compatible providers (`/v1/models/{id}`), Anthropic and Gemini (`/v1beta/models/{id}`) return the provider's model object in Bifrost's shape; an upstream 404 is a 404 and a failed call a 502. Other providers keep the stub `{id, owned_by, listed}` from the known list. L105: Cohere's retrieve (`providers/cohere.tov` modelRetrieveRequest, `model_retrieve.tov` retrieveBody: Bifrost's model with its name, context length, deprecation and endpoints as supported methods). Vertex, Azure and Bedrock keep the list entry, as Bifrost has no retrieve for them (its ModelRetrieve is unsupported in `core/providers/vertex/vertex.go:4552`, `azure/azure.go:2705` and `bedrock/bedrock.go:4358`), so there is no provider answer to read. |
| POST /v1/mcp/tool/execute | handlers/mcpinference.go | done | `server.tov` `dispatch` → `gateway.tov` `Engine.executeTool`. |
| GET /v1/realtime (WebSocket upgrade) | handlers/wsrealtime.go | done (OpenAI, Azure, xAI) | `server.tov` `wsUpgrade` → `realtime.tov` `prepare` and `upstreamFor` (the `realtimeKind` set, as Bifrost's RealtimeProvider; Azure sends Entra ID tokens and the configured headers, L108). The socket is a `RealtimeSocket` on both servers (L128): its frames go through the same `LiveCore` (`webrtc.tov`) as a WebRTC session, so each turn (a `response.create`, a server-detected `input_audio_buffer.committed` or a `response.created`, up to `response.done`) is admitted again by governance and a refusal ends the session with an error event; the text of both sides is guarded (the client's item, instruction and function output text before the provider gets it, output text deltas, and the transcript of what the caller said); `response.done` usage is billed once per response (audio tokens at the audio rates, cached tokens at the cache rate, the rest at the text rates, `pricing.tov` `usageParts`); and each turn is a log row of its own, as Bifrost logs them (L137: `op` realtime, written and charged when its `response.done` arrives, with `realtime_transport`, `realtime_session_id`, `realtime_unit` and `realtime_unit_index` and the audio and cached token counts of that turn in its metadata, what was said in the turn as input and output, `parent_request_id` and the metadata `session_request_id` naming the session, as Bifrost's turn context sets the session as the parent request); a turn refused at its start, a connection that broke and a turn cut off are one more row (`realtime_unit` end) with the status and error. Frames pass as they came except where a guard redacted text. A `response.done` whose status is failed is logged with the status Bifrost gives a realtime error (500, 400 for an invalid request, 429 for a rate limit or budget) and its message, a cancelled one with 499 (Bifrost's cancelled status), an incomplete one as 200 (Bifrost maps none of them; an incomplete answer was delivered), each with `realtime_response_status` and `realtime_status_reason` in its metadata and its tokens billed (L160, `webrtc.tov` `doneOutcome`). Transcription sessions (L160): `?intent=transcription` is upgraded before its model is known; the first `session.update` (`session.audio.input.transcription.model`, or the beta `transcription_session.update`) names it within Bifrost's bootstrap limits (16 frames, 1 MiB, 15 s), the session is admitted for it, the provider's `/v1/realtime?intent=transcription` (Azure `/openai/v1/realtime?intent=transcription`; xAI refused, as Bifrost) gets the held frames with the model pinned bare, and each `conversation.item.input_audio_transcription.completed` is a row (`realtime_unit` transcription) billed from its usage at the transcription rates (audio and text tokens, else the seconds of a duration usage; `livehooks.tov` `transcriptionUnitCost`). A client secret this gateway minted is accepted (Authorization or the `openai-insecure-api-key.` subprotocol) as the virtual key it was minted under, names the model when the URL does not, runs on the pool key that minted it and sends the provider its own token (`rtsession.tov` `realtimeCredential`). Open: a second `response.create` during a response is not refused with "Conversation already has an active response"; binary frames are dropped. The upgrade answers with the `realtime` subprotocol when the client offered it (OpenAI and Azure; none for xAI), never the credential-bearing one, as Bifrost's upgrader does (`realtime.tov` `answerProtocol`, on both servers). ElevenLabs is a media provider here (out of scope, G38). |
| POST /v1/realtime/calls (WebRTC SDP) | handlers/webrtcrealtime.go | done (OpenAI, Azure) | `server.tov` `dispatch` → `realtimecalls.tov` `realtimeCall` (L128): terminated on Raiju's own peer (`webrtc.tov` `establish`, as `POST /v1/live/sessions` is). The multipart `sdp` and `session` fields (the model from `session.model`, bare, and the nested transcription models too, as `StripNestedModelPrefixes`), or a raw SDP body with `?model=`, are read; governance admits the call and the credential is checked; the browser's offer is answered by the gateway's peer, the provider is sent the gateway's own offer (a multipart POST to `/v1/realtime/calls` for OpenAI, `/openai/v1/realtime?model=` for Azure, under the provider key) and the audio RTP and the `oai-events` channel are relayed through the same `LiveCore` as the WebSocket (turns admitted, text guarded, tokens billed, one row, `realtime_transport` webrtc). Answers 200 `application/sdp`; a provider's refusal is passed on as it came. xAI has no WebRTC realtime API (400), as in Bifrost. A minted client secret (`Bearer ek_bf_...`) is the virtual key it was minted under and the provider gets its own token (L160); a session of type transcription keeps no top-level model, its input transcription carries the routed one, and it is billed per transcription. L184 (owner decision after wave 22): off unless `server.webrtc_enabled` is true (Raiju's key; Bifrost has no switch), a 403 before the body is read (`live.tov` `webrtcRefusal`), for this route, `POST /v1/realtime` and the WebRTC create of `/v1/live/sessions`; WebSocket realtime is unaffected. |
| POST /v1/realtime (WebRTC SDP offer at the base path) | handlers/webrtcrealtime.go (resolveRealtimeSDPTarget) | done | `server.tov` `dispatch` → `realtimecalls.tov` `realtimeCall` (the beta format: a raw SDP body with `?model=`, or the multipart fields; the provider is called at its `/v1/realtime?model=` as Bifrost's `ExchangeLegacyRealtimeWebRTCSDP` does, for Azure at `/openai/v1/realtime`). GET still upgrades to the WebSocket (`server.tov` `wsUpgrade`). |
| POST /v1/realtime/client_secrets | handlers/realtime_client_secrets.go | done | `server.tov` `dispatch` → `realtimecalls.tov` `realtimeMint` (L160), as Bifrost's handler: JSON only; the model from `session.model`, the top-level `model` or a transcription session's input transcription; admitted by governance under the caller's virtual key; minted with a pool key at OpenAI `/v1/realtime/client_secrets`, Azure `/openai/v1/realtime/client_secrets` or xAI `/v1/realtime/client_secrets` with the body normalized (bare model, session type, xAI's connection-only body); the provider's token is replaced by the gateway's own `ek_bf_` token, whose mapping (virtual key, pool key, sealed provider token, model) is kept on the cluster state until the provider's expiry (`rtsession.tov`), so every worker and node honours it. A provider answer with no token is a 502, a refusal is passed on. The mint is not logged or billed (as Bifrost). |
| POST /v1/realtime/sessions, /v1/realtime/transcription_sessions | openapi (legacy alias) | done | `realtimecalls.tov` `realtimeMint` (L160): OpenAI's older mint routes, minted and mapped the same way, the token nested under `client_secret` replaced (Bifrost's parser takes that shape; its router mounts client_secrets only). Other providers are refused on them, as Bifrost's Azure mints on client_secrets only. |
| /v1/live/sessions (REST, WebSocket attach, content) | handlers/livecontrol.go, wslive.go | done | `server.tov` `wsUpgrade` → `live.tov` `LiveSocket` (session.start and attach); `live.tov` `contentId` and `liveContent` (recording). OpenAI only (`live.tov` `attachUpstream`), as in Bifrost (its LiveProvider is OpenAI alone). Bifrost has no list route: its GET /v1/live/sessions is the WebSocket primary (`handlers/wslive.go:48`), served here by `wsUpgrade`. POST /v1/live/sessions (WebRTC create) is terminated here, as Bifrost's webrtclive.go does (`live.tov` `liveCreate`, `webrtc.tov` `establish`, `Relay` and `LiveCore`; L122): the browser's offer is answered by a peer connection of this gateway (libdatachannel, tov-patches/0006), a second peer connection offers to OpenAI with the gateway's own offer, the audio crosses as RTP packets and the `oai-events` channel as text, and the session is recorded for the virtual key. What is metered is listed in the "Live sessions (WebSocket and WebRTC create)" row. The live registry is shared between worker processes (L102); a WebRTC session stays on the worker that took it. |
| POST /v1/async/{op} (submit) | handlers/asyncinference.go; docs/features/async-inference.mdx | done | `server.tov` `dispatch` → `asyncSubmitHooked` (`webhooks.tov`); `jobs.tov`. Ops: chat/completions, completions, embeddings, responses, images, audio, rerank, ocr and messages. L184: job ids are 128 random bits from the kernel (`job_` + 32 hex; Bifrost's are random UUIDs), and the job records its owner (`jobs.tov` `jobOwner`: the virtual key, else a hash of the credential sent). |
| GET /v1/async/{op}/{job_id} (Bifrost poll paths) | handlers/asyncinference.go; async-inference.mdx "Supported Endpoints" | done | `server.tov` `asyncPoll` → `extras.tov` `asyncGet`; `jobs.tov`. Statuses are pending, processing, completed and failed. L184: a job submitted with a virtual key is shown only to that key (Bifrost's RetrieveJob), one submitted with another credential only to it (Raiju is stricter here), and an anonymous one to any poll; another caller's job is a 404 (`JobStore.getFor`). The same applies to x-bf-async-id polls. |
| GET /v1/async/jobs/{id} | not in Bifrost | done (Raiju-only) | `extras.tov` `asyncGet`. |
| POST /v1/count_tokens | not in Bifrost openapi | done (Raiju-only) | `server.tov` `dispatch` → `extras.tov` `countTokens`. Exact for Anthropic and Gemini, estimated (`"exact": false`) elsewhere. |
| POST /v1/decisions (and /openai/v1/decisions) | handlers/inference.go; docs/providers/self-hosted-decision-models.mdx | done | `server.tov` `dispatch` → `extras.tov` `decisionRoute` (`decisions.tov`, `decision_emulate.tov`). Custom providers and TypeSafe answer on their base format; OpenAI, Azure, Cloudflare, Databricks, Perplexity and Ollama on their own decision routes (L163, `providers/decision_native.tov`); other providers are refused, as Bifrost refuses them. The unprefixed `/decisions` route is served under `/openai` and the SDK prefixes (`server.tov` compatPath, sdkPath; L109). Decision pricing overrides (`pricing.tov` priceFor, decisionCost; L109). L173: `/openai/v1/decisions` (and `/openai/decisions`, and the same under `/litellm`, `/langchain`, `/pydanticai`, which mount Bifrost's OpenAI routes) speaks OpenAI's Decisions API as Bifrost's route does: the request is exactly OpenAI's (no input, a structured input, Typesafe's top-level state, no questions, and a field a question, choice or level does not define are refused by name with a 400, `decisions.tov` openaiRequestProblem, Bifrost `ToBifrostDecisionRequest`), a model with no provider prefix is OpenAI's (`extras.tov` openaiModel), and the answer is rendered in OpenAI's shape whichever provider answered (no id, name null on an unnamed answer, usage as input_tokens, output_tokens and total_tokens; `decisions.tov` openaiResponse, Bifrost `ToOpenAIDecisionResponse`). `/v1/decisions` keeps Bifrost's normalized shape. Open (L81): the x-bf-passthrough-extra-params gate. |

Six prefixes done, including GenAI interactions as raw passthrough. `/runware_passthrough` is not built: Runware is out of scope (SCOPE.md; owner decision 8 in HANDOFF.md). |
| /typesafe/v1/models, /typesafe/v1/systemone | integrations/typesafe.go | done | `server.tov` `dispatch` → `extras.tov` `decisionRoute` (`providers/typesafe.tov`). |
| /agents/a2a/{name}/* (agent card, json-rpc, rest message, tasks, push configs) | handlers/agentgateway.go | partial | `agents.tov` (card, grants), `a2a.tov` (operations, bindings), `a2apush.tov` (push relay) and `ops.tov` `a2aRoute`/`a2aCall` (L116, L134). GET .../.well-known/agent-card.json serves the cached card with its interfaces rewritten to the gateway's JSON-RPC and REST routes, push advertised only when the agent can push, signatures dropped, `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`, an ETag and 304. POST .../json-rpc takes the v0.3 and the v1 method names: message/send and message/stream, tasks/get, tasks/list, tasks/cancel, tasks/resubscribe (SubscribeToTask), the four push-config methods and the extended card. .../rest/... takes Bifrost's HTTP+JSON routes (message:send, message:stream, tasks, tasks/{id}, tasks/{id}:cancel, tasks/{id}:subscribe, tasks/{id}/pushNotificationConfigs[/{id}], extendedAgentCard, an optional tenant segment), with REST errors in the {error:{code,status,message,details}} shape. A call is sent to the card's interface of the caller's binding, else to the other one, and the answer is mapped back (results, errors, event streams), so a REST client reaches a JSON-RPC-only agent and the reverse. Push configs: create/delete go to the agent with the gateway's ingress URL and a token of its own, get/list answer from the gateway's store, POST .../push/callback takes the agent's pushes by that token and queues them for the relay (6 attempts, 1 s doubling to 5 min, application/a2a+json with the caller's token and credentials), a config embedded in a send is handled the same way. The runtime headers are fixed headers or an OAuth bearer token; the caller's other headers go through; with forward_accepted_credential the caller's gateway credential goes on the header it came in on. L171: the A2A-Version header is checked on the JSON-RPC and REST bindings after the caller's credentials (`a2aver.tov` versionSupported): none, any 1.x and 0.3 (the older dialect Raiju also serves) are spoken, anything else is refused as Bifrost refuses it (JSON-RPC -32009 "this version is not supported" with the request's id, REST 400 FAILED_PRECONDITION). The caller's A2A-Version is not forwarded; a v1 interface gets its own protocolVersion. Bodies are translated between v0.3 and v1 both ways, as a2a-go's a2acompat/a2av0 converts them (parts, roles, task states, the kind tags and the v1 wrappers, a status update's final, blocking and returnImmediately, embedded and stand-alone push configs, the push get, list and delete params and answers, tasks/list keys), in every binding and stream: a JSON-RPC caller speaks the version of its method names, REST and gRPC callers v1, and each interface the version it declares (v0.3 REST routes under /v1, its extended card at /v1/card). The interfaces a call may use (`a2agrpcup.tov` upstreamCandidates, interfaceProblem, as Bifrost's isStrictV1Interface) are those of a protocol version the gateway speaks (a v1 card's interfaces name 1.x or 0.3, a v0.3 card's speak 0.3, a v0.3 gRPC interface is not called), a binding it calls, the URL guard, and no plaintext endpoint (http://, or a plaintext gRPC target) on a card fetched over https unless on the loopback interface; a card with none is refused. They are tried with the caller's binding first and the newest version first; a failure that proves the interface unusable (no connection, 404, 405, 410 or 426 to a POST without a REST error body, method not found, gRPC UNIMPLEMENTED) moves the call to the next one, and those and the softer ones (a 5xx to a POST, a transport failure, gRPC UNAVAILABLE, INTERNAL, UNKNOWN, DEADLINE_EXCEEDED) send later calls to the other interfaces first until the failed one works again or the card changes (attemptVerdict, Demotions; Bifrost's retryableEndpointFailure and interfaceFailure). Cancellation: a stream's caller that goes away (Bun server), a gRPC client's RST_STREAM or lost connection, and a gRPC call past its grpc-timeout cancel the call upstream: the HTTP request to the agent is aborted, its gRPC stream reset (unary gRPC calls go as streams so they can be reset; `a2a.tov` CallCancel, `h2.tov` H2Body.onReset). Extensions (`a2aext.tov`, a registration's extension_uris): the served card and the extended card list only the allowed ones; an agent that requires a denied one (or declares one twice, or requires one without a URI) is not relayed (503); a caller's A2A-Extensions naming one not relayed is -32004, a required one left out -32008, a requested extension's metadata over 64 KiB -32602; the header goes on to the agent. The extended card is served as the gateway serves the agent's card. A declared agent's card is fetched at start (one process of a node) and, still missing, on the agent's first call in every binding (Bifrost discovers it lazily); a call is refused 503 only when that fetch fails (not tried again for 5 s). Grants (`governance.tov` agentAccess): besides the agent's virtual_key_ids and allow_by_default, a key's agent_grants, the agent_grants of its active access profiles (its own and its user's), and a project the call names, combined by its access_rule (a project unknown or not admitting the caller falls back to the caller's grants, as Bifrost documents). L176: a refused grant is answered as Bifrost's governance PreA2AHook refusal reaches each binding (core/agent/manager.go gateOutcomeError, a2a-go's ErrUnauthorized): on JSON-RPC, once the call is read, error -31403 in a 200 with the request's id, the governance message ("Agent 'x' is not allowed") and data holding the google.rpc.ErrorInfo (reason UNAUTHORIZED, domain a2a-protocol.org, metadata agentName, requestId, requestType as Bifrost names it, send_message and so on, and timestamp); on REST 403 PERMISSION_DENIED with the same ErrorInfo in details (and the path's taskId); on gRPC PERMISSION_DENIED with the ErrorInfo in grpc-status-details-bin; on the card route Bifrost's bare `Forbidden` 403 (`ops.tov` `grantDenied`, `grantInfo`; `a2a.tov` `unauthorizedInfo`, `grantDeniedRpc`, `grantDeniedRest`, `requestTypeOf`). An unsupported A2A-Version is still refused first. The A2A plugin pipeline: Bifrost's only A2A plugins are governance (the grant decision of its PreA2AHook, built into the gate here) and logging (the history rows, built in here); the WebAssembly ABI has no A2A hooks and Go .so plugins are owner decision 1, so no plugin kind Raiju loads can take part. L194: a v0.3 REST client's routes under /v1 (message:send, message:stream, tasks, tasks/{id}, tasks/{id}:cancel, tasks/{id}:subscribe, tasks/{id}/pushNotificationConfigs[/{id}], and card for the extended card) are calls in the v0.3 dialect (`a2a.tov` restCallV03): their bodies are read as v0.3 params (a push config as {pushNotificationConfig} or the config alone), the call goes to the agent's interface translated as for a v0.3 JSON-RPC caller, and the answers come back in v0.3 (kinds, lower-case states, v0.3 push views; `ops.tov` a2aSendOn). A v1 call under a tenant named "v1" is now read as v0.3 when its next segment is one of v0.3's routes; e2e `l194_e2e`. gRPC binding (L138; the transport is L142, the codec L143, the binding L148): Bifrost serves the A2A v1 gRPC service (handlers/agentgatewaygrpc.go, the SDK's `a2agrpc` handler over the agent's request handler) on a shared listener of its own, takes the agent from the first label of the request's `:authority` (`<agent>.<server.a2a_grpc_base_domain>:<server.a2a_grpc_port>`, agent names up to 63 characters), authenticates each call with the virtual-key rules and advertises the interface on the served card. L142 added the HTTP/2 transport: `h2.tov` (frames, a plaintext server connection with prior knowledge, flow control both ways, streams, trailers, the stream limit and GOAWAY and RST_STREAM answers, and the client that `grpc.tov` uses), `hpack.tov` (HPACK with Huffman coding, the dynamic table and never-indexed literals) and `grpcwire.tov` (gRPC messages, status trailers, grpc-timeout, the HTTP to gRPC status table), tested by `tests/h2_echo.tov` and `tests/h2_test.py` with grpcio and the h2 package. `protobuf.tov` encodes the OTLP messages and is also the generic wire codec (L143: a bounds-checked reader, zigzag, fixed and float values, strict UTF-8), and `a2apb.tov` maps the lf.a2a.v1 messages (the requests and responses of the eleven methods and the types they reference, with Struct, Value, Timestamp and Empty) to and from protojson's JSON. Its bytes and JSON are checked against a2a-go v2.5.0 golden vectors (`tests/fixtures/a2apb_golden.json`, made by `tests/fixtures/a2apb_golden.go.txt`), byte for byte in both directions. The binding (L148, `a2agrpc.tov`) serves the eleven methods on the A2A dispatch on its own listener, when server.a2a_grpc_base_domain and server.a2a_grpc_port are both set: the agent is the first label of the :authority under the domain (a foreign host, no agent or more labels is NOT_FOUND "agent not found"); the virtual key comes from the metadata in Bifrost's order (x-bf-vk, a Bearer sk-bf- token, x-api-key, x-goog-api-key, api-key), and a key that was sent but is unknown is UNAUTHENTICATED even where auth is not enforced; the call then passes the gate of the HTTP bindings (`Ops.a2aGrpcGate`, which needs client.a2a_external_client_url as Bifrost does). Each method decodes its request (a2apb), runs the operation the JSON-RPC binding runs (`Ops.a2aCall`, binding "grpc", the agent's card choosing the names it goes out in), and encodes the answer; a JSON-RPC error is the gRPC status a2a-go maps it to, and a refusal is mapped from its HTTP status. A grpc-timeout bounds the call and its stream reads. Logs and governance are those of the HTTP bindings. Cards list the gRPC interface (`<agent>.<domain>:<a2a_grpc_port>`, in supportedInterfaces of a v1 card, additionalInterfaces of a v0.3 one) when the listener is configured. L161: an error the agent answered carries the google.rpc.Status details a2a-go sends (grpcutil ToGRPCError, `grpcstatus.tov`): grpc-status-details-bin (unpadded base64) with a google.protobuf.Struct for each structured detail of the JSON-RPC error's data (the first one twice, as a2a-go writes the error's Details and then its typed details) and a google.rpc.ErrorInfo with the A2A reason of the code (TASK_NOT_FOUND, SERVER_ERROR, ...), the domain a2a-protocol.org and the string metadata of the data's ErrorInfo; the gateway's own refusals stay plain statuses, as Bifrost's are. Upstream gRPC (L161, `a2agrpcup.tov`): a call goes to the card's interface of the caller's binding, else to the first interface the card lists that the gateway can call (Bifrost tries a card's interfaces in order), so an agent whose card offers only GRPC, or lists it first, is called over gRPC from the JSON-RPC, REST and gRPC bindings. The interface is a dial target as Bifrost's grpcUpstreamTarget reads it (host:port or https:// is TLS with ALPN h2 and the agent's runtime_auth.tls, http:// is h2c; an explicit port, no path, query or credentials; the URL guard of the HTTP bindings applies, so plaintext needs allow_private_network), on the shared HTTP/2 client of `h2.tov` (not Tov patch 0007, whose fetch carries no trailers). The params are encoded as the method's request message (`a2apb.tov`; a v0.3 push config create and delete are brought to the v1 messages), the forwarded headers and runtime credentials go as metadata, unary calls are bounded at 60 s, a stream at 300 s until its head; the answer becomes the JSON-RPC answer the dispatch maps for every binding (a gRPC status is the JSON-RPC error a2a-go's FromGRPCError reads: the code from the ErrorInfo's reason and the status code, the message, and data with the Struct details and the ErrorInfo), and each StreamResponse one event as it arrives, a failed trailer the last event. A card is accepted when it offers only a gRPC interface the gateway may dial. L190: the TLS client certificate of runtime_auth and discovery_auth (client_cert_pem and client_key_pem, both or neither: Bifrost's upstreamTLSConfig, whose message a half pair fails every call with) is presented on the card fetch, the OAuth token calls and every binding's calls, gRPC over TLS included (`agentauth.tov` tlsOf, `h2.tov`; Tov patch 0015); an HTTP caller (JSON-RPC or REST) that goes away before a unary answer stops the call upstream, as the gRPC binding's caller does (`clientgone.tov`: Bun.serve's req.signal, the http1 server's socket close, fired into the call's CallCancel); gRPC messages in grpc-encoding gzip are read on the binding and the answer goes back in gzip, as grpc-go's server answers with gzip registered (Bifrost links it through its OpenTelemetry exporters), another encoding is UNIMPLEMENTED `grpc: Decompressor is not installed for grpc-encoding "x"` with grpc-accept-encoding gzip, a message over the limit after decompression RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED, the compressed flag under identity INTERNAL (`grpcwire.tov`); upstream calls offer grpc-accept-encoding gzip and read gzip answers (`a2agrpcup.tov`); e2e `l190_e2e`. Open: no TLS on the listener (h2c, as Bifrost's); a stream caller's disconnect on the http1 server; with bun workers (-workers above 1) one worker opens the listener after the fork, the one that takes the lease (a mkdir in the node's share folder whose pid file names the holder; a lease whose holder is gone is taken over on Linux only, when the replacement starts), and every worker's cards advertise it. The listener is not opened in the parent: a socket opened before the fork was accepted but not read by the forked workers (Tov's TCP listeners have no SO_REUSEPORT). When the holder dies the gRPC listener stays down until a worker takes the lease again; the runtime's supervisor did not replace a killed worker in our test, so a gateway restart is the way back. Bifrost fetches the card on each request; Raiju keeps the cached card and fetches it again on a timer (client.a2a_card_refresh_seconds) and, at most once a minute, when the agent answers as if its endpoint had moved. L149: a card fetched by one worker or node is published under the state's namespace `a2a.card` (the URL, the fetch time and the card) and adopted by the registrations for that URL that hold an older card or none, so no worker answers 503 for an agent another one has; the timer runs in every worker, one worker of a node takes each period's turn (a FileLease in the node's share folder, `takePeriod`), and a node fetches the cards of the agents it owns (`ownerOf` over the live nodes), the other nodes adopting theirs from the state. L148b: a card a worker fetches is written to the node's share folder at once (`Ops.publishCards`, also after a save through the agents API), so every worker of the node holds it within one share tick (100 ms, `shareTimer`); a call that names an agent whose card this worker does not hold reads the share folder first (`Ops.heldAgent`, `Ops.syncShared`), then fetches it (L171), and is refused 503 in every binding only when that fails (`a2aGate`, the card's message), which the gRPC binding maps to UNAVAILABLE; before this an unfetched card was an internal error over gRPC. Push configs and queued pushes are shared by every worker of a node (L138, `a2apush.tov` `PushStore`): one file per config and per queued push in the `a2a-push` folder of the logs_store folder (the workers' share directory when there is no logs store), each written in one write, so workers changing different items never lose each other's writes, and every method reads the folder first, so a registration taken by one worker is seen by the others and a push may reach any worker. A queued push is sent by one worker at a time: `claim` takes a lease on it (a directory made with mkdir, which only one process can make), reads the push again once it holds the lease so a push another worker just finished is not sent twice, and `release` gives the lease up after the outcome is written; the lease of a worker that died is taken over after 60 s as a new generation, and a holder that stalled past its lease does not overwrite the new holder's outcome. Delivery stays at least once (a worker that dies after the callback answered but before it wrote the outcome sends again). Across the cluster (L149, `a2ashare.tov`, on `clusterstate.tov` from L147): push configs travel under the state's namespace `a2a.push`, with the callback token and credentials sealed under the encryption key the nodes share, so a callback registered through one node is listed and answered by every node, an agent's push is accepted at any node's ingress, and a deletion (a tombstone) reaches them all. A queued push stays with the node that received it and is delivered once there. A config a node held before the state reached it is not sent until it changes (Bifrost's config database holds all of them). Not covered: a node away longer than the ten-minute tombstone window can bring back a deletion it missed; without an encryption key the callback secrets travel in clear (the cluster wire is signed, not encrypted). Within a node, one folder is shared by the workers (the lease is an mkdir, which a POSIX file system makes atomic). A single-file `a2a-push.jsonl` left by an earlier version is moved into the folder at the first start. L188: an HTTP call to the agent follows a redirect only on the origin it was sent to, at most ten, as Bifrost's `clientFor` does (`redirects.tov` `sameOriginRule`, `ops.tov` a2aSendHttp; before, every redirect was followed); card fetches, OAuth endpoints and push callbacks follow none; every connection to the agent is checked by the runtime under its `allow_private_network` (`tov-patches/0013`). The gRPC interface (raw sockets) is checked by the URL guard before each call only. |
| GET /agents/a2a (aggregated card) | not in Bifrost | done (Raiju-only) | L134, `agents.tov` `aggregateCard`, `ops.tov` `a2aAggregate`: one A2A card for the gateway listing the agents the caller's key may call, their skills under their names, and each agent's card, JSON-RPC and REST routes. Also at /agents/a2a/.well-known/agent-card.json. |

### a3. Management API (/api)

Bifrost: `transports/bifrost-http/handlers/*.go`, documented in `docs/openapi/openapi.json`. Raiju routes: `server.tov` `manage` (dispatches the /api tree), `store.tov` `handleApi` (config, providers, governance, roles), `ops.tov` (users, logs, MCP logs, audit, sessions), `mcpapi.tov` (MCP clients), `profiles.tov`, `prompts.tov`, `webhooks.tov`, `flags.tov`, `notify.tov`, `alertapi.tov`.

| PUT /api/config (client_config, framework_config, auth_config), POST /api/config/metadata | handlers/config.go | done | `store.tov` `putConfig` (L97j): every given section is checked before any is saved. client_config as before (refuses unknown settings); framework_config is `frameworkPlan` (pricing_url, pricing_sync_interval become `pricing_sync`; empty pricing_url resets; model_parameters_url and live_models_sync_interval are kept, L180, with Bifrost's checks; the section's MCP library settings are kept); auth_config is `authPlan` (admin_username, admin_password with "<redacted>" kept, is_enabled false clears the credential, disable_auth_on_inference sets client.enforce_auth_on_inference), saved by `ConfigStore.setAdminAuth`. POST /api/config/metadata is `store.tov` `postMetadata` (L97b), stored as the top-level `metadata` map (strings, numbers, bools; null removes; objects and lists refused). Bifrost's setup_token and the one-time bootstrap rules of auth_config are not applied (the management API is already behind the admin gate). |

Bifrost's list shape (logs, pagination, stats, has_logs), with Raiju's entries and total alongside. Filters: providers, models, status, virtual_key_ids, team, customer, user, business unit and project ids, session_id, user_agents, apps, objects, min and max latency, tokens and cost, missing_cost_only, content_search, request_id, start_time, end_time, period, sort_by and order (L97c), and since L97l selected_key_ids, routing_rule_ids, routing_engine_used, stop_reasons, aliases, parent_request_id, roots_only, group_sessions, agent_correlation_id, complexity_tiers, complexity_mechanisms and cache_hit_types, recorded by `gateway.tov` `record` from the request labels and `reqlog.tov` `stopReasonOf`. L97t adds tool_call_names (`reqlog.tov` `toolCallNamesOf`: the names a whole answer called, from chat tool_calls, Responses function_call items, Anthropic tool_use blocks or Gemini functionCall parts, first seen first, recorded whether or not content is logged, as Bifrost does), min_complexity_score and max_complexity_score (the heuristic's score, `complexity_score` on the row, present only when the heuristic classified the request; Bifrost has no such query filter), and selected_prompt_ids and prompt_names (the prompt named with x-bf-prompt-id, recorded with its version as `selected_prompt_id`, `selected_prompt_name` and `selected_prompt_version`; Bifrost stores these columns but has no query filter for them). agent_names (L116) selects the requests in the sessions (A2A context ids) of the calls to the named agents, within the window; agent_names is applied on the list and stats routes, not on the histograms. Streamed answers record their tool names as their events go out (`reqlog.tov` `ToolNameStream`, L107; L168 adds Gemini stream chunks). L168: content_search also matches the message text of the stored request and answer, ignoring case, as Bifrost's content summary (`logstore.tov` `contentTextOf`: the content, text, input, prompt, instructions, system, query and delta strings of a JSON body or of a streamed answer's data lines; JSON keys and roles are not text), and the log reads leave out client.hidden_request_types (see that row). L174: a streamed answer's stop reason is recorded too, the last one its events carried as the client got them (a chat chunk's finish_reason, an Anthropic message_delta's stop_reason, a Gemini chunk's finishReason; `reqlog.tov` ToolNameStream.stop, `gateway.tov` after the stream), and for a large answer passed through, from its kept tail (`reqlog.tov` stopReasonOfTail); a cached stream's is read from its events (`reqlog.tov` stopReasonOf); e2e `l174_e2e`. Open (owner decision): complexity mechanism values are Bifrost's plus "heuristic" (Raiju's own analyzer) and "override" (x-bf-complexity-tier), and the routing engine is "routing-rule" or "loadbalancing" (a key picked by weight), not Bifrost's "governance". |

The session id is recorded on each entry from x-bf-session-id or a harness session header (`affinity.tov` `sessionId`, whose header list is shorter than Bifrost's: no x-session-affinity or session-id). Session pages are capped at 500, oldest first. |
| GET /api/logs histograms (cost, tokens, latency, models, throughput, by provider or dimension), rankings, dashboard, filterdata, dropped | handlers/logging.go | done | `ops.tov` `logsRoute` (`logstore.tov`). L110: the overall latency histogram carries avg, p90, p95 and p99 overhead (`overhead_ms`, from the gateway's overhead). Model rankings group by provider and model, and each row carries throughput (successful rows with a latency, as Bifrost's tokensPerSecond) with its trend, input, output and additional cost, and canonical_model_name when an alias sets one. Trends follow Bifrost's pctChange and metricTrend (null for a zero base that moves). The by-dimension histograms take Bifrost's names team_id, customer_id, user_id, business_unit_id and project_id, echo the name asked for, and answer Bifrost's 400 messages; rankings by dimension take team, customer, business_unit, project, user, virtual_key, app and user_agent. Raiju's own dimensions (provider, model, virtual_key, tool, server) stay accepted on both routes without being listed in the messages. Log rows carry canonical_model_name and overhead_latency. Limits: recalculate-cost reprices the total only, so the cost split keeps the value from the request; a streamed answer sent straight to the client has no overhead recorded. filterdata adds user_ids, apps and user_agents. /api/logs/rankings/users added (L97c). |
| DELETE /api/logs; POST /api/logs/recalculate-cost (status, cancel) | handlers/logging.go | done | `ops.tov` `deleteLogs` (admin only: all-data scope), `recalculateCost` (202, or 409 with the job in flight), `recalcStatus` (?id=, else the job in flight, else idle), `cancelRecalc`. The job is `costrecalc.tov` `CostRecalc`: it walks the selected logs `RECALC_SLICE` at a time between timer ticks, with total, processed, updated, skipped and message (L97l); cancel keeps what was priced. Costs are priced again from the current price list (`pricing.tov`). L168: a log whose model the current price list does not know keeps its cost and is counted in `unpriceable` (a subset of skipped, with Bifrost's completion message), rather than being priced at 0. With several workers the job's status is kept in `recalc-cost.json` in the node's share folder (`costrecalc.tov` `share`, `readShared`), so a status poll on any worker answers for it, a second start on any worker gets the 409, and a cancel on another worker asks the running one to stop before its next slice (`requestCancel`; the answer says the cancellation was requested). A status file not written for two minutes is a dead job. A job is still run by one process, not resumed after a restart (Bifrost resumes from its database cursor). DELETE /api/logs also deletes a hidden row's retained object (see retain_content_in_object_storage). |
| /api/logs/user-agent-mappings CRUD | handlers/logging.go | done | `ops.tov` `userAgentRoute` → `logapi.tov` `uaRoute`; saved in `config.tov` `user_agent_mappings` (via `store.tov` `setUserAgentMappings`). The app of a request is the first active mapping that matches, then Bifrost's built-in identifiers, else Other (`logapi.tov` `appOf`). L168: match_type regex works as Bifrost's compiled mappings: the pattern runs unanchored and case-sensitive (a `(?i)` flag in the pattern makes it insensitive) on the User-Agent through `regex.tov` compileRegex, compiled once per pattern (`logapi.tov` `uaMatches`); a pattern that does not compile is refused with 400 "failed to create user agent mapping: invalid user agent mapping: invalid regex pattern: ...". |

Authorization-code OAuth clients are done (L91); the per_user_oauth and per_user_headers flows are done (L97k, L97o). verify-exchange is done for token_exchange clients (L112b, the auth type row). verify-exchange keeps the exchanged token as the client's admin credential, and the client connects with it (L133, see the token exchange row); its refresh token renews it, and a refused renewal shows needs_reauth until verify-exchange runs again (L140). Every route Bifrost registers in `handlers/mcp.go` is served (`mcpapi.tov` VERIFY_ROUTES); a route that does not apply to a client's auth type answers Bifrost's refusal. |

A spec_url sent over the API is fetched when the client is created or updated and stored with it, spec_url kept as provenance (L166: `mcpapi.tov` inlineSpecUrl, wired in `server.tov`), as Bifrost's prepareOpenAPIConfig does; with dashboard auth off only a public address is fetched. |

Library: `mcplibrary.tov` (rows in `<config>.mcp_library.json`), `ops.tov` libraryRoute and configureLibrary: GET with search, category/connection_type/auth_type/tags filters, sort and paging (25 by default, at most 100), filterdata, POST a custom server (409 on a taken slug), DELETE hides a row for good, force-sync; synced from `framework.pricing.mcp_library_url` (Bifrost's default URL when unset; `file://` read) at start and every `mcp_library_sync_interval` seconds on the leader (a day by default, 0 never), custom and hidden rows protected, as `modelcatalog/mcp_library_sync.go`. Opt-in as the price sync: it runs when `pricing_sync` names `mcp_library_url` or is enabled; without a config file GET answers empty and the writes 503. L188: the catalog URL is checked as Bifrost's `ValidateExternalURL(url, true)` and its private-network client check it: private addresses may be reached, link-local, unspecified and metadata ones never, on the literal (`MCP library URL validation failed: ...`) and on every address the host or a redirect's resolves to when the runtime connects (`mcplibrary.tov` fetchCatalog). |
| /api/mcp/virtual-mcps CRUD, virtual-key attach and detach | handlers/virtualmcp.go | done | `mcpapi.tov` `virtualRoute`; `vmcp.tov`. |

| /api/mcp/sessions (list, reauth, revoke); /api/oauth/per-user/flows/* | handlers/mcpsessions.go | done | `mcpapi.tov` `perUserApi` (L97o): the flow detail and start (JSON authorize_url, PKCE), the sessions list (token rows, pending flows, header credentials; paged, `kind` filter), reauth (new consent link) and revoke. The token rows are `mcp_config.user_tokens`, sealed at rest (`store.tov` transformSecrets). Bifrost's list shape is partial (no user or DAC fields; times are epoch milliseconds). Flows are process-local, as the authorization code flows are. |
| /api/mcp/per-user-headers flows and credential revoke | handlers/mcpheaders.go | done | L97k: `mcpapi.tov` `createFlow`, `flowDetail`, `submitFlow` (async `headersRouteAsync`), `revokeCredential`. Flows are opened through POST /api/mcp/per-user-headers/flows, and (L167) by the tool call itself as Bifrost opens them from the inference error: a key that has submitted nothing is answered 401 with "Authentication required for <client>. Visit <link> to submit the required headers." (`mcpapi.tov` `headersAuthMessage`, `gateway.tov` `toolGate`), the link being the auth page `/workspace/mcp-sessions/auth?flow=<id>&kind=headers`, which reads the flow and submits the values (`mcpconsent.tov`), with an `mcp_headers_auth` temp token in the fragment when client.mcp_enable_temp_token_auth is on (GET and PUT of that flow only, ended when the submission completes or the credential is revoked). Credentials are per virtual key in `cfg.mcp.user_credentials`, sealed by `store.tov` `transformSecrets`. L167: the admin credential kept for tool sync (the verify-headers sample, which the client connects with) is listed as Bifrost's credential block (kind headers, the header names, active or needs_update when a declared header has no sample value; the client then reads state needs_reauth; `mcpapi.tov` `ownCredential`). L177: credentials and flows are keyed by the caller's identity, as Bifrost's (mode, identity) rows: a virtual key (vk mode), an x-bf-mcp-session-id (session mode, `session_id`) or the user of a user-mode MCP token (user mode, `user_id`) (`mcp.tov` `McpUserCredential`, `McpHeaderFlow`, `userHeadersFor`; `gateway.tov` `headersIdentity`, `toolGate`). A tool call with no identity is refused with Bifrost's "requires an identity" message; the flow detail answers flow_mode with virtual_key, session_id or user_id; a user-mode flow opens and submits only for its own single sign-on user and mints no temp token (Bifrost's canAccessUserFlow); revoke and GET /api/mcp/sessions (auth_mode) follow the identity; POST .../flows (Raiju's) also takes session_id. The identity precedence is the per-user OAuth one (see the x-bf-mcp-session-id row). e2e: mcp_peruser_e2e (a session's own values beside its key's). |
| /api/oauth/callback | handlers/mcpoauth2.go | done | `server.tov` `dispatch` → `mcpapi.tov` `oauthCallback` (MCP authorization-code flow, PKCE). |
| /api/oauth/config/{id}/status, DELETE /api/oauth/config/{id} | handlers/mcpoauth2.go | done | `mcpapi.tov` oauthConfigRoute (the config is the MCP client of auth type oauth). L167: status is authorized (with token_expires_at), failed (its last verification flow failed and it holds no token) or pending; with ?flow_id= (initiate-verification and reauthorize answer flow_id and status_url, a reauthorize's status_url carrying it) the status is the flow's, as Bifrost's resolveOAuthFlowPollStatus answers it (`pollStatus`): pending until the callback, expired past the flow's deadline, failed when the authorization server refused it (an ?error= callback) or the token request failed, and once the flow is done the config's status; a flow of another config is 404. The admin flows are kept in the flow state file with the per-user flows (`mcpuser.tov` `startAdminFlow`, `failAdminFlow`, `endAdminFlow`), so a restart keeps them. oauth_config_id is the client's name. DELETE drops the tokens; a live session keeps its token until it reconnects. |
| /api/oauth2/sessions; /api/oauth2/consent/flows/* | handlers/mcpoauth2sessions.go, mcpoauth2consent.go | done | L97r: `mcpissuer.tov` `consentDetail` and `consentSubmit` serve GET and PUT /api/oauth2/consent/flows/{id} with the consent temp token (the fragment of the consent link); the page is `mcpconsent.tov` at /oauth/consent. L132: the consent and connect pages use the admin UI's look, and the admin UI lists and revokes the grants (MCP access tab). L106: `grantsList` and `grantRevoke` serve GET /api/oauth2/sessions (the live refresh grants, filtered by q, bf_mode, virtual_key_id and user_id, paged; the id is the SHA-256 of the grant's current refresh token) and DELETE /api/oauth2/sessions/{id} (revokes the grant, 204). Session mode: `consentModes` offers it to a dashboard user signed in on the request while inference auth is not enforced; a session grant runs anonymously and keys its per-user OAuth tokens by the session id. L167, user mode on Raiju's OIDC sign-in (`oidc.tov`; Bifrost's identity resolver): while SSO is on, consent offers "user", GET answers logged_in_user (id and email) for the single sign-on session sent as the Authorization bearer (`ops.tov` `consentContext`), and PUT {"mode":"user"} grants a token with bf_mode user and the user's subject (400 "no active session; please sign in first" without one); a key with a user_id grants user mode for that user and only to that user signed in (Bifrost's VK user upgrade). The consent page has its own sign-in: it offers the gateway's OIDC sign-in (/api/oidc/login?return_to=, back to the consent page, the temp token kept in the tab's sessionStorage), then "Continue as <user>". A user-mode grant is listed by user_id. |
| /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource, /.well-known/oauth-authorization-server, /.well-known/jwks.json, /oauth2/register, /oauth2/authorize, /oauth2/token | handlers/mcpoauth2discovery.go, mcpoauth2issuance.go | done | L97r (`mcpissuer.tov`, on when client.mcp_server_auth_mode is both or oauth): the discovery documents (RFC 9728, RFC 8414), the JWKS, RFC 7591 registration of public clients, authorize with PKCE S256 and the RFC 8707 resource, and the token endpoint (authorization_code and refresh_token, rotation, family revocation on reuse). Access tokens are RS256 JWTs (iss, aud = the /mcp resource, sub = the virtual key id, the session id or the user, bf_mode vk, session or user); `ops.tov` mcp maps them to the key, the session or the user. L106: the signing key is the configured PEM or a 2048-bit key generated at the first start and kept in `mcp-oauth-state.json` beside config.json (sealed with the config's encryption key when one is set). Registered clients and refresh rows are kept in that file and read again on each refresh. L167: the pending authorization requests, their consent tokens (as SHA-256 hashes) and the consented codes (as hashes, with the request) are kept there too and read back when a worker does not hold one, so a restart, or another worker, between authorize, consent and the token request keeps the flow; a code is still single use. disable_vk_identity works as Bifrost's, while user mode is available: consent offers no vk mode, /mcp refuses vk-mode tokens ("virtual-key identity is no longer accepted; re-authenticate") and their refresh is refused (invalid_grant). A user-mode token is refused at /mcp and at refresh once governance lists its user as disabled, and when a dashboard session on the same request names another user; an OAuth token sent with a virtual key header is refused as conflicting credentials. As in Bifrost, an access token of a revoked grant stays valid until it expires; the state file is rewritten whole on each change (atomically), and revoked rows are dropped after 30 days. |
| /api/session/login, logout, is-auth-enabled | handlers/session.go | done | `ops.tov` `sessionRoute`. |
| /api/session/setup, /api/session/ws-ticket | handlers/session.go | done | ws-ticket: `ops.tov` `sessionRoute` → `wsticket.tov` `WsTickets` (30 seconds, single use, issued to a caller who may read the logs); `/ws?ticket=` is consumed in `server.tov` `wsUpgrade`. setup: `ops.tov` `sessionRoute` trades the setup token for the setup session cookie while no admin exists, and answers 409 once one does (L97l). L172: Bifrost's setup lock (handlers/middlewares.go SetupLockMiddleware): while no admin account exists and setup_token is set, every /api route but the public ones (is-auth-enabled, login, logout, setup, the OAuth callback, version, the SCIM OAuth routes, served skills) needs `X-Bifrost-Setup-Token` (a wrong one is a 403 `invalid setup token`) or the setup session cookie (401 without either), and the caller then acts as the admin (`ops.tov` `setupLock`, `setupsession.tov` `setupPublic`, `sameSecret`); the UI socket and the ws-ticket route take the cookie too. e2e `l97g_e2e`, `l97l_e2e`. L184 (owner decision after wave 22, reversing L97g): with no setup token either, the API is locked as Bifrost's (every /api call but the public ones a 403 `dashboard auth is not configured and no setup token is set`), the token is setup_token or else the `BIFROST_SETUP_TOKEN` environment variable (Bifrost's resolveSetupToken; `ops.tov` `setupToken`), and start-up warns while the lock is on (`ops.tov` `startupWarnings`). e2e `l184_e2e`. |
| /api/oidc/login, /api/oidc/callback, /api/oidc/status | not in Bifrost openapi | done (Raiju-only) | `ops.tov` `oidcRoute`. |
| /api/audit-logs (list), /export, /verify | enterprise/audit-logs.mdx; openapi | done | `ops.tov` `auditRoute`; `audit.tov` `find`, `filterData`. L97c adds /api/audit-logs/{id} (Bifrost's {audit_log} wrapper; the id is the event's sequence number) and /api/audit-logs/filterdata (actors, actions, methods, paths, ips) and the /api/governance/audit-logs alias (`server.tov` `auditTarget`, RBAC in `rbac.tov`). L168: the list answers Bifrost's AuditLogsResult (`audit.tov` `auditListJson`: audit_logs as CADF events with id, typeURI https://getbifrost.ai/schemas/auditlog.json, eventType activity, eventTime, action, outcome, initiator, target, reason for a failure, the masked body as an attachment, requestMethod, requestPath, requestIP; total, page, limit, total_pages, has_more, next_cursor), with Raiju's events alongside. It takes Bifrost's filters (`auditQueryOf`): page, limit (default 100, at most 1000), cursor, search, action(s), outcome(s), event_type(s), initiator_id(s) and _type(s), target_id(s) and _type(s), request_method(s), request_path(s) (prefixes), request_ip(s), tags, start_date and end_date (RFC 3339 or a date, the end date to the end of its day), period, sort_order; a plural is a JSON array and wins over its singular. The CADF action of a write is create, update or delete (authenticate for a login, access otherwise); the outcome is success below 400, else failure; the initiator type is api_key, session, system or user, the target type comes from the action or the route. GET /api/audit-logs/{id}/verify (and the alias) computes the event's HMAC again from its stored fields and compares it in constant time: {valid, computed, stored}; an event that left memory is read from the file. filterdata adds Bifrost's dimensions (actions, outcomes, event_types, initiator_types, target_types, request_methods, initiators, targets, request_paths, request_ips, tags) with dimensions= and q=. With omit_ip_addresses, addresses are left out of every answer and the ip filters and search skip them. Raiju's events carry no tags and are all activity events. |
| /api/alerts (GET) | not in Bifrost openapi | done (Raiju-only) | `ops.tov` `alertsView`. |

| /api/plugins CRUD, /builtins, /loaded | handlers/plugins.go | done | `ops.tov` `pluginsRoute` lists GET /api/plugins and /api/plugins/{name} (from the config's plugins, with status from `plugins.tov` `entryStatus`), /builtins (the Bifrost names Raiju implements) and /loaded (the live chain). L97l: POST /api/plugins (201, 409 when the name is taken, 400 for an unknown name or a config that does not build), PUT and DELETE /api/plugins/{name} (`ops.tov` `createPlugin`, `updatePlugin`, `deletePlugin`); each save goes through `store.tov` `setPlugins` and `engine.reload`, which rebuilds the live chain (`plugins.tov` `buildChain`). L121: POST and PUT take `path` (a local `.wasm` file) and `wasm_limits`; the list shows `path` and `isCustom`, and `/loaded` names a WASM plugin by the name its `get_name` returns. L170: POST and PUT read `placement` (`pre_builtin` or `post_builtin`, else Bifrost's 400 "Invalid placement value. Must be 'pre_builtin' or 'post_builtin'") and `order` from the body (`ops.tov` `placementOf`, `orderOf`), and the listing shows them; a `path` that is an http or https URL is downloaded first (`plugindl.tov`, see Dynamic plugins), and a download that fails is Bifrost's 500 "Plugin was not created, it failed to load: ..." (PUT: "Plugin update was not applied, ..."); PUT on a plugin that is not configured creates it (off unless the body turns it on), as Bifrost creates the row; a custom plugin `path` while dashboard auth is off is Bifrost's 403 ("... requires genuine admin authentication ..."), and a built-in plugin's `path` is dropped; the mocker's list entry carries its statistics (`stats`, Bifrost's GetStats). Design (owner decision 1 after wave 14): a `.so` path is refused with the reason (no Go plugin loader). L175 (`pluginmerge.tov`): PUT merges the body's config over the stored one by top-level key, and a redacted placeholder (a masked string or SecretVar object) keeps the stored value, as Bifrost's restoreRedactedFromExisting (a string in an array is a list entry); while dashboard auth is off, a POST or PUT whose config adds or changes an endpoint (collector_url, metrics_endpoint, push_gateway_url, endpoint, url, base_url, without case) or an env./vault. reference is refused with Bifrost's 403 naming the paths (`dialTargets`, `changedTargets`, Bifrost's pluginConfigDialTargets and requireGenuineAuthForPluginConfigChange); keeping or removing one passes. |
| /api/feature-flags | handlers/featureflags.go | done | `server.tov` `manage` → `flags.tov` `flagRoute`. |
| GET /api/cache (stats) | handlers/cache.go | done | `server.tov` `cacheView`. DELETE /api/cache/clear/{id} and /clear-by-key/{key} are in `ops.tov` `cacheClear` (L97c): they clear the exact-match cache (by the SHA-256 hex of the key, or the x-bf-cache-key the entry was stored under) and the semantic cache's local memory. Shared cache files are removed. L168: another worker drops its memory copy of a cleared exact-match entry on its next lookup (`cache.tov` get: a copy whose shared file is gone was cleared), and the semantic cache logs each clear in its share folder (`semcache.tov` `clears.log`), which the other workers apply at their next sync, deleting the shared entry files too. A semantic hit's id (the hash its x-bifrost-cache-id carries) clears it on every worker. With an external vector store, clear/{id} deletes the store's entry and clear-by-key deletes every entry stored under the key (`vectorstore.tov` `deleteWhere`, Bifrost's DeleteAll by cache_key; new entries carry cache_key) on Qdrant, Redis and chromem; a store error answers 500 "Failed to clear cache". L175: clear-by-key on Weaviate (a batch delete by the cache_key property, `DELETE /v1/batch/objects`, Bifrost's ObjectsBatchDeleter; a class missing a tag property gets it added on open) and Pinecone (a delete by metadata filter, Bifrost's DeleteVectorsByFilter, which reports no count), and a Redis index created before L168 gets its missing TAG fields with FT.ALTER on open (`vectorstore.tov`). L192: a clear that comes before any request opened the namespace opens it as it stands, with no semantic_cache.dimension (`vectorstore.tov` `attach`: Qdrant's collection, Redis's FT.INFO, Weaviate's class, Pinecone's index stats, chromem's namespace file whose first entry gives the dimension; `gateway.tov` vectorClear); a namespace that does not exist holds nothing to clear, and the next lookup still ensures it. |
| /api/circuit-breaker/policies (CRUD), /state, /reset | openapi | done | `server.tov` `manage` → `ops.circuitPoliciesRoute` (`health.tov`), `ops.circuitView`, `ops.circuitReset`. Policies persisted (L91). |
| POST /api/vault/flush-cache | openapi | done | `server.tov` `manage` → `ops.vaultFlush`. |
| POST /api/cluster/sync (peer sync), GET /api/cluster/leader, POST /api/cluster/config | Bifrost has its own cluster gossip (`cluster_config`) | done | `ops.tov` `clusterSync` (Raiju HMAC protocol). Not wire-compatible with Bifrost clustering. GET /api/cluster/leader is Raiju-only (`ops.tov` `clusterLeader`, L94). POST /api/cluster/config (L185, `ops.tov` `clusterConfig`) hands a peer with `cluster.config_sync: "pull"` the config text of a generation this node holds; signed over a body with the generation and the time, refused when older than a minute. Differs internally: Bifrost's open-source HTTP server registers no /api/cluster routes (its nodes sync over their own gossip wire), so this route is part of Raiju's cluster wire; what a cluster shares is in (i) Clustering. L195: every route is signed with a `cluster.secret` that is resolved from `env.NAME` and must hold at least 32 bytes (the start is refused otherwise; `cluster.verify` refuses an empty key); with `cluster.listen_port` the routes are served on a cluster listener of their own (`server.tov` `clusterDispatch`, `startCluster`; `front.tov` `startClusterFront` on http1) and answer 404 on the main listener, as Bifrost keeps its gossip on separate ports. |
| /api/prompt-repo/* (folders, prompts, versions, sessions, deployments) | handlers/prompts.go | done | `server.tov` `manage` → `prompts.tov` `promptRoute`. Deliveries and deployments (L88). |
| /api/skills/* and /api/skills/serve/* (marketplace git, zip) | handlers/skills.go, skills_serving.go | out-of-scope | Skills marketplace is in SCOPE.md Out. |
| /api/agents/* (history, push-configs, inspect) | handlers/agentgateway.go | done | `agents.tov` `agentApi` and `server.tov` manage (L116): GET and POST /api/agents, GET, PUT and DELETE /api/agents/{name} (PUT replaces, a masked secret keeps its stored value), POST /api/agents/inspect (fetches a card without saving), POST /api/agents/{name}/refresh-card, and GET /api/agents/history (`ops.tov` `agentHistoryRoute`: the A2A calls, including push_notification and push_delivery rows, newest first, with agent_names, limit and offset; no stats). L134 adds GET /api/agents/push-configs (`ops.tov` `pushConfigsRoute`: agent_names, task_id, config_id and url filters, limit 25 to 100, offset; secrets never shown; the same on every node since L149) and DELETE /api/agents/push-configs/{agent}/{task}/{config} (204; the gateway's copy only). L171 (`agenthistory.tov`, `ops.tov` agentHistoryRoute): the list takes Bifrost's filters (agent_name, operation, user_id, virtual_key_id, team_id, customer_id, business_unit_id, project_id, status, task_id, context_id, request_id, search, start_time and end_time in RFC 3339, sort_by timestamp or latency, order) with its 400 messages, and its rows carry operation, record_kind, task_id and the two transports; DELETE /api/agents/history ({ids}), GET .../stats (total_entries, success_count, error_count, success_rate, average_latency), .../histogram (success and error per bucket, Bifrost's bucket ladder, every bucket of an asked range), .../top-agents (ten), .../filterdata (users, virtual keys, teams, customers, business units and projects with their names; dimensions and q) and .../{id} (Bifrost's AgentLogDetail: names, task, context, transports, latency, the request and response bodies with credentials redacted, raw_request and raw_response) answer in Bifrost's shapes, 503 without a logs store. Registrations take tenant and extension_uris (checked and stored sorted as Bifrost does). L175 (`agenthistory.tov` historyPageJson, groupedCalls, hydratedJson): the list answers Bifrost's {logs, pagination} (limit, offset, sort_by, order, total_count) and 503 without a logs store; group_sessions keeps each context's earliest call with session_child_count (not when context_id, task_id or request_id is given, as SessionGroupingActive); hydrate answers each call's detail with its events, on the list and on .../{id}. L192 (`ops.tov` recordA2aEvent, `agenthistory.tov` a2aEventOf, eventsOf, requestOf, `a2a.tov` StreamTap.watch): each event a streamed call sends its caller is a row of its own (record_kind event, Bifrost's ObserveA2AEvent and describeA2AEvent) under the call's request_id: event_sequence (1-based), event_type (message, task, status_update, artifact_update, read from a v1 StreamResponse member or a v0.3 kind, in a JSON-RPC answer or a REST payload), task_state, message_id, artifact_id, content_type (the first part's media type) and the event itself (event_body, kept when the call's content is); the list takes record_kind, event_type and task_state (a call matches through its events, by the latest reported state for task_state, as Bifrost matches child rows), group_sessions and hydrate list calls only, hydrate carries each call's events oldest first, .../{id}?hydrate answers the operation a row belongs to (Bifrost's FindAgentLogOperation), and top-agents counts calls only (stats and histogram count what the filter selects, events included, as Bifrost's do). Calls and their events carry the business unit of the caller's team and the project the request named when it admits the caller (`governance.tov` attributionOf), and the list rows show request_id and the user, team, customer, business unit and project ids. The list keeps Raiju's agent_names and method fields beside Bifrost's. |
