| retain_content_in_object_storage | client.retain_content_in_object_storage | done | Built (L125). When content logging is off for a request (client.disable_content_logging, a virtual key's override, or x-bf-disable-content-logging with client.allow_per_request_content_storage_override), its input, output and error go to `logs_store.object_storage` (type s3 or gcs, with the bucket, keys, endpoint and path_style the log exports take) as a hidden JSON object at `<prefix>/logs/YYYY/MM/DD/HH/<request id>.json`, prefix default "bifrost", `.json.gz` when object_storage.compress is set. Raw provider payloads stay out, as Bifrost keeps them only while content logging is on. The log row keeps `content_hidden: true` and no bodies, and neither the list nor GET /api/logs/{id} ever reads the object back, as Bifrost's hidden content. Uploads run from a bounded queue (`retained.tov`: 5,000 objects, 64 MiB), once a second, at most 16 a pass, five tries each with growing pauses; a failure never fails the request, and a given-up object is counted and logged. Without a bucket the option is refused at startup and content-off requests keep nothing, as Bifrost. e2e `l125_e2e` (the mock as the bucket: key, row, detail, outage and retry). L168: DELETE /api/logs deletes a hidden row's object (both names, .json and .json.gz), as Bifrost's DeleteLogs; a failed delete is only logged. The retention prune leaves the objects to the bucket's lifecycle rules, as Bifrost's DeleteLogsBatch does. The bucket and the credentials may be given as a secret reference object ({value, type, ref, env_var, from_env}; `config_compat.tov` `mapStorageRefs`: env.NAME, vault.<ref> or the value). object_storage_exclude_request_types and _fields never apply to hidden rows in Bifrost either (a hidden row always uploads its whole payload). L193: the hybrid store (`hybrid.tov`, Bifrost's framework/logstore/hybrid.go): with logs_store.object_storage set, every LLM row's payload fields (input, output, raw_request, raw_response, plugin_logs) go to the bucket as one object at `<prefix>/logs/YYYY/MM/DD/HH/<id>.json` (`.json.gz` with compression gzip or the legacy compress, `.parquet` with format parquet: a one-row file, a string column per field), queued and retried like retained content; the row keeps its metadata, error (Bifrost keeps error_details in the row) and a preview of the last user message as its input, with `has_object`; GET /api/logs/{id} reads the object back (every key its format may have given it, JSON, gzip or Parquet) and merges it, as Bifrost's hydrateLog, while the list, stats and histograms read the rows only; object_storage_exclude_fields (Bifrost's column names or Raiju's) keeps fields in the row, object_storage_exclude_request_types keeps whole rows (Bifrost's type names or Raiju's ops); DELETE /api/logs deletes a row's object; hidden rows go the retained way only. Differences: Parquet pages are uncompressed or GZIP (Raiju has no snappy or zstd codec: those settings are written as GZIP, with a warning; Bifrost's default for parquet is snappy), MCP tool rows stay whole, and `logs_store.object_storage_offload: false` (Raiju only) keeps rows whole. Behaviour change: an existing object_storage now offloads payloads (Bifrost's behaviour). e2e `l193_e2e` (the mock as the bucket, json gzip and parquet). |
| webhook_config | client.webhook_config | done | Deliveries are the last 500 (`webhooks.tov`), persisted in the logs_store folder when logs_store is on (L97i). delivery_history_retention_days, the only key of Bifrost's webhook_config (`configstore/tables/clientconfig.go` WebhookConfig, config.schema.json), is applied (default 30 days); per-endpoint tuning lives on each webhooks[] entry. |

L106: the signing key is generated and kept in the state file when none is configured (see the discovery row above). |
| mcp_external_client_url, skills_external_client_url | client.* | out-of-scope | The skills marketplace is in SCOPE.md Out (L88). MCP external URLs are reported as not implemented. |
| a2a_external_client_url, a2a_store_raw_request_response | client.* | done | a2a_external_client_url is the public base URL the gateway's cards, endpoints and push ingress advertise (`config.tov` client; `agents.tov` publicBase drops a trailing slash, and an unset value falls back to the request's origin, where Bifrost answers 503 until it is set). a2a_store_raw_request_response (L171, `ops.tov` recordA2a, `agenthistory.tov` rawCapture): each call's log row keeps the bytes sent to the agent and the bytes it answered, base64, a direction too big dropped (up to 768 KiB, so its base64 fits the log store's 1 MiB bound; Bifrost keeps 1 MiB); a stream's when it ends; none for a gRPC upstream, as Bifrost captures the HTTP transports only. Shown by GET /api/agents/history/{id}. |
| client.a2a_card_refresh_seconds | not in Bifrost | done (Raiju-only) | L134, `ops.tov` `startCardRefresh`/`refreshCards` and `agents.tov` `refreshAll`: how often the registered agents' cards are fetched again (default 300, 0 for none); a changed card is saved. A card is also fetched again, at most once a minute, when its agent answers HTTP 405, 410, 426, or 404 without a protocol error. Runs only in the process that may save the config (not in the workers of a multi-worker run). |

### c3. providers.*

Bifrost: `$defs/provider`, `base_key`, `network_config`, `custom_provider_config`, `proxy_config`, `azure_key`, `bedrock_key`, `vertex_key`, `openai_config`, `prompt_cache`. Raiju: `src/config.tov` (`KeyConfig`, `NetworkConfig`, `ProxyConfig`, `CustomProviderConfig`, `ProviderConfig`) and `src/config_compat.tov` (`providerChildKeys`, `keyChildKeys`).

| Feature | Bifrost | Raiju status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| keys[]: name, value, models, weight, aliases, blacklisted_models | base_key | done | `config.tov` KeyConfig; `router.tov` pickKey. blacklisted_models (L173): request model names (alias names for aliases) the key never serves, "*" for all; it wins over models and aliases in routing and in the listing (`router.tov` keyBlocks, listingPipeline). |
| keys[].use_for_batch_api | base_key | done | `media.tov` providerTarget: file and batch routes use only keys with this set; a provider with keys and none flagged answers 404 (L97g). Existing configs need `use_for_batch_api: true` on the keys that serve batches or files. |
| keys[].oauth_key_config | base_key | done | `oauth_key.tov` builds the token request and reads the answer, as Bifrost's tokencache (L172): client_credentials sends the client's id and secret as HTTP Basic auth (Bifrost's default auth_style `header`) or in the form (`body`), with scope, audience and the extra parameters (sorted); jwt_bearer signs an RFC 7523 assertion with iss (`issuer`), sub (`subject`, else the issuer), aud (`audience`), iat and nbf backdated 30 seconds, exp after `assertion_lifetime_seconds` (300 by default, up to 3600), a random jti and `kid` from `key_id`, by `signing_algorithm` (L180: every algorithm Bifrost lists): RS256, RS384 and RS512 (RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5) and PS256, PS384 and PS512 (RSA-PSS with MGF1 and a salt as long as the hash) with an RSA key (`jwt.tov` signRsa), ES256, ES384 and ES512 with an EC key on P-256, P-384 or P-521, SEC 1 or PKCS#8 (`ecdsa.tov` signEs, RFC 6979 nonces, checked against the RFC's A.2.5 to A.2.7 vectors), over SHA-256, SHA-384 and SHA-512 (`crypto.tov`). `gateway.tov` `oauthToken` mints the token for a key with an empty value, caches it per credential (every field that changes the token, Bifrost's oauthKeyCacheKey) and mints a new one before it expires; it is applied on the routes' attempt loop, the raw passthrough routes (`passthrough.tov`), and the file, batch and media calls (`media.tov`, small and large uploads). token_url, client_id, client_secret and private_key may be env.NAME. Token URLs must be https, or loopback http, with no userinfo, query or fragment. A key is refused as Bifrost's OAuthKeyConfig.Validate refuses it (`oauth_key.tov` oauthKeyProblem): a jwt_bearer key needs its private_key, issuer and audience (no fallback), and an algorithm from the list; the key routes (POST/PUT /api/providers/{p}/keys, PUT /api/providers/{p}) refuse such a key with 400, and a literal PEM that does not fit its algorithm (an RSA key for ES*, an EC key on another curve) with Bifrost's keyMatchesSigningAlgorithm words. The key routes keep every per-key block on save (oauth_key_config and the azure, vertex, bedrock, bedrock_mantle, watsonx, cloudflare, databricks, github_copilot and replicate blocks), mask each block's secrets on read, and keep a stored secret sent back masked (`store.tov` keyWithSecrets; they used to drop the blocks). e2e `l112a_e2e`, `l180_e2e` (PS256 and ES512 assertions checked against the keys' public halves). |
| keys[].azure_key_config (endpoint, deployments, api_version) | azure_key | done | `config.tov` AzureKeyConfig. |
| Azure Entra ID: client_id, client_secret, tenant_id, scopes | azure_key (docs/providers/supported-providers/azure.mdx) | done | `config.tov`; `cloud_auth.tov`. Client credentials, tokens cached (commit 6aeb7f2). |
| bedrock_key_config (access key, secret key, session token, region, deployments) | bedrock_key | done | `config.tov` BedrockKeyConfig. Mantle keys (`bedrock_mantle_key_config`) are read too (2c31e06). L190: a key with neither an API key (value) nor an access and secret key signs with the AWS SDK's default chain, as Bifrost's signAWSRequest does (`awschain.tov`: the environment, EKS IRSA web identity, the shared credentials file, ECS and EKS Pod Identity container credentials, the IMDSv2 instance profile), on chat, files and batches and the model listing; role_arn is then assumed with those credentials; a chain with none fails the call with "failed to retrieve aws credentials". |
| Bedrock: role_arn, external_id, session_name | bedrock_key | done | Assumed through STS (`config.tov`, commit 6aeb7f2). |
| Bedrock: inference-profile arn, use_openai_endpoints | bedrock_key | done | `config.tov` BedrockKeyConfig; `providers/bedrock.tov` (the arn joins the model in the URL path; use_openai_endpoints selects OpenAI and xAI endpoints) (commit 2c31e06). |
| Bedrock: batch role and S3 | bedrock_key | done | `media.tov` batch_role; batches and files over S3 (commit e572dc3). |
| vertex_key_config (project_id, region, auth_credentials) | vertex_key | partial | `config.tov` VertexKeyConfig. L181: aws_workload_identity (audience, service_account_email, token_lifetime_seconds, aws_region, aws_role_arn; env. values read) federates the workload's AWS identity into a GCP token as Bifrost's vertex/awsfederation.go does (`vertexwif.tov`, `cloud_auth.tov` awsSubjectToken, gcpStsRequest, impersonationRequest): an STS GetCallerIdentity request signed with SigV4 for the regional STS host of the region's partition, bound to the pool provider by x-goog-cloud-target-resource and serialized as GCP replays it, exchanged at sts.googleapis.com for a federated token, and with a service account exchanged again at IAM Credentials generateAccessToken for the lifetime asked (3600 s by default); aws_role_arn is assumed first (session bifrost-vertex-federation). An AWS external_account credentials file in auth_credentials (gcloud's create-cred-config --aws, environment_id aws1) runs the same flow with its own token URL, impersonation URL and lifetime. Tokens are cached by their settings until a minute before they expire, on the chat path and the media, file and batch routes; e2e `l181_e2e` against mock STS and IAM endpoints. project_number serves tuned models at the project's v1beta1 endpoint (L172, L173: `providers/vertex.tov` isEndpointModel, vertexModelUrl, also per alias); the us and eu regions use their multi-region hosts (L172 vertexHost). L174: a multi-region-only partner model (the datasheet's vertex_multi_region_only, copied as a fixed list: claude-opus-4-7, claude-opus-4-8, claude-opus-5) in a us-* or europe-* region is called in its pool, us or eu (the rep.googleapis.com host and the location in the path), count-tokens included, unless force_single_region (the key's, or an alias's) keeps the configured region (`providers/vertex.tov` effectiveRegion, isMultiRegionOnly, regionPool; Bifrost getVertexEffectiveRegion); e2e `l174_e2e`. L190: the AWS credentials come from the SDK's default chain as config.LoadDefaultConfig builds it (`awschain.tov`): the environment, an EKS IRSA web identity (AWS_WEB_IDENTITY_TOKEN_FILE and AWS_ROLE_ARN exchanged at STS AssumeRoleWithWebIdentity, AWS_ROLE_SESSION_NAME), the shared credentials file's profile, container credentials (ECS task roles and EKS Pod Identity: AWS_CONTAINER_CREDENTIALS_RELATIVE_URI or _FULL_URI with the SDK's host rule, AWS_CONTAINER_AUTHORIZATION_TOKEN_FILE or _TOKEN) and the instance profile through IMDSv2 (IMDSv1 when the token call is not served); temporary ones are cached until a minute before they expire; the region is aws_region, AWS_REGION, AWS_DEFAULT_REGION, then the instance metadata's (placement/region), as Bifrost's resolveRegion reads it; AWS_ENDPOINT_URL_STS, AWS_EC2_METADATA_SERVICE_ENDPOINT and AWS_EC2_METADATA_DISABLED are honoured; e2e `l190_e2e` against mock STS, container and IMDS endpoints. Open: the shared config file (~/.aws/config: profiles with role_arn, credential_process, SSO, its region) is not read; a check against a real AWS-to-GCP federation is EXTERNAL. |
| watsonx_key_config | not in Bifrost | done (Raiju-only) | `config.tov`. |
| network_config: base_url, extra_headers, default_request_timeout_in_seconds, max_retries, retry_backoff_initial, retry_backoff_max | network_config | done | `config.tov` NetworkConfig; `router.tov`. |
| network_config: insecure_skip_verify, ca_cert_pem | network_config | done | `providers/types.tov` tlsOf (commit b66cf96). |
| network_config: allow_private_network | network_config | done | L186: a `base_url` saved through `POST /api/providers` or `PUT /api/providers/{p}` is checked as Bifrost's `ValidateExternalURL` checks it (`store.tov` `baseUrlProblem` on the literal, `providerBaseUrlProblem` with the host resolved, called from `server.tov`): http or https with a host, loopback always allowed, link-local, unspecified and metadata addresses never (in any spelling, `netguard.tov`), other private addresses only with `allow_private_network`; a host that does not resolve is refused (400 `Invalid base URL: ...`). L188: every provider connection is checked when it is made, as Bifrost's `ConfigureDialer` checks each dial: the runtime (fetch's `peer` option, `tov-patches/0013-fetch-peer-policy.patch`) checks every address the host resolves to then, for a `base_url` from the config file or the API, key-level URLs, model listings, live sessions and the token endpoints a key configures (watsonx `iam_url`, Copilot's GitHub API), each request, redirect and new pooled connection (`providers/types.tov` `peerOf`, `netguard.tov` `providerPeer`): loopback allowed, link-local, unspecified and metadata addresses never, other private addresses only with `allow_private_network`. A refused connection fails the request (`Connection to a private address ... is not allowed`). Behind a provider proxy nothing is checked, as Bifrost's dialer skips its checks behind a proxy. Not checked: realtime WebSocket connections to providers (raw sockets, no runtime check). |
| network_config: enforce_http2, http2_ping_interval_in_seconds, keep_alive_timeout_in_seconds, max_conns_per_host | network_config | done | Applied per provider through the Tov fetch pool options (`tov-patches/0007`, L126): `providers/types.tov` `poolOf` maps them (defaults 30 s and 5000 as Bifrost; ranges 1 to 3600, 1 to 10000, ping 0 to 3600) and every provider request passes the result as `fetch`'s `pool`. Idle connections close after the keep-alive; at most `max_conns_per_host` connections to the host, further requests wait their turn (per process: a `workers: n` server has a pool per worker). `enforce_http2` offers h2 in the TLS handshake and multiplexes requests as streams on one connection (nghttp2 1.64.0 vendored in the Tov patch); an origin that does not pick h2 gets HTTP/1.1, as Go's ForceAttemptHTTP2 does for Bifrost, and plain `http:` origins are always HTTP/1.1. `http2_ping_interval_in_seconds` sends a PING after that long without a frame (only with `enforce_http2`; a PING unanswered for 15 s closes the connection). Bifrost applies enforce_http2 and the ping interval to its net/http providers only (Bedrock); Raiju applies them to every provider. Tests: `tov-patches/tests/fetch_pool.tov` (23 checks), unit `poolOf`, e2e `l126_e2e`. |
| network_config: beta_header_overrides | network_config | done | L127. `anthropic_beta.tov` is Bifrost's anthropic-beta filter (FilterBetaHeadersForProvider and MergeBetaHeaders): a known flag goes upstream only where the provider supports its feature (Anthropic's API all; Vertex, Bedrock and Bedrock Mantle the subsets Bifrost's ProviderFeatures lists), an unknown flag only to Anthropic, the fallback-credit date is rewritten on Bedrock and Mantle. The overrides by prefix (`config.tov` NetworkConfig.beta_header_overrides) decide first. Applied to the upstream headers of Claude requests in `gateway.tov` (kinds anthropic, vertex, bedrock, bedrock_mantle; the provider's `extra_headers` flags merge with the caller's), to `anthropic_batch.tov` passthrough and to `media_native.tov` anthropicHeaders. The native Messages route (`/anthropic/v1/messages`) forwards the caller's anthropic-beta by default, as Bifrost's Anthropic integration does. Raw passthrough (`passthrough.tov`) forwards it unfiltered, as Bifrost's Passthrough does. Not done: Bedrock's body-side `additionalModelRequestFields.anthropic_beta`. e2e `l127_e2e`. |
| network_config: stream_idle_timeout_in_seconds | network_config | done | Every upstream stream the gateway reads is cut when a read waits longer than the value: the converted and passed-through streamed answers (`gateway.tov`, the client gets the stream's error event), the answer of a large request that streams back, and the raw passthrough routes (`passthrough.tov`, the body ends) (L172). Unset or 0 means Bifrost's DefaultStreamIdleTimeoutInSeconds, 120 seconds (core/schemas/provider.go). Only the wait for the upstream counts, so a client that reads slowly is not taken for a silent upstream; one interval checks every live stream (`deadline.tov` `IdleWatch`, `idleWatch`, `streamIdleSeconds`) instead of a timer per chunk. e2e `l112a_e2e`. |
| concurrency_and_buffer_size | provider.concurrency_and_buffer_size | done | `gateway.tov` limitersFor; `limiter.tov` (per-provider concurrency and queue). |
| proxy_config (provider): http with credentials and ca_cert_pem | proxy_config | done | `config.tov` ProxyConfig; `providers/types.tov`. Bifrost's schema allows only type `http`. Other types give no proxy (`proxyOf`). |
| send_back_raw_request, send_back_raw_response | provider.* | done | `gateway.tov` sendBackRaw (commit cbd6ad3). |
| store_raw_request_response | provider.store_raw_request_response | done | `gateway.tov` storeRawOn; overridden per request when `client.allow_per_request_content_storage_override` is on. |
| ignore_provider_cost | provider.ignore_provider_cost | done | Honoured (L97q): when true, a provider's usage.cost is ignored and the request is priced from the table (`gateway.tov` serve). When false, usage.cost is the billed cost of an OpenAI-format chat answer (OpenRouter), as in Bifrost. L105: a streamed chat answer is billed the same way, from its usage chunk (`streams.tov` OpenAIPassthrough, `gateway.tov` serve's direct outcome, and `providers/openai.tov` streamResponse for a buffered stream). |
| injected_tools | provider.injected_tools | done | `gateway.tov` injectedNames and injectedDef: the provider's MCP tools join each chat request it serves. Needs MCP clients (startup note). |
| custom_provider_config.base_provider_type, is_key_less | custom_provider_config | done | `config.tov`; `router.tov` kindOf. |
| custom_provider_config: request_path_overrides | custom_provider_config | done | `providers/openai.tov` pathOverride. |
| custom_provider_config: allowed_requests | custom_provider_config | done | `gateway.tov` allowedRequest; a fallback takes a request type the provider does not allow. |
| custom_provider_config: does_not_send_done_marker, wait_for_usage | custom_provider_config | done | `gateway.tov` (the stream ends at finish_reason when `does_not_send_done_marker` is set without `wait_for_usage`). |
| openai_config.disable_store | provider.openai_config | done | `providers/openai.tov` (store is forced to false when set). |
| prompt_cache (provider): auto_inject, ttl, cache_control_injection_points | provider.prompt_cache | done (Anthropic) | Raiju has a top-level `prompt_cache.auto_inject` (`config.tov` PromptCacheConfig). Provider-level ttl (5m or 1h) and cache_control_injection_points (message role or index) are applied by `providers/anthropic.tov` messagesBody; a request that carries its own cache_control gets no injected marker (L97g). Other providers ignore them. |

### c4. governance.*

Bifrost: `config.schema.json` governance properties. Raiju: `GovernanceConfig` in `src/config.tov`, consumed by `src/governance.tov` and `src/store.tov`.

| Feature | Bifrost | Raiju status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| budgets, rate_limits, customers, teams, virtual_keys, routing_rules | governance.* | done | Core fields done. VK `mcp_configs` (`virtual_key_mcp_config`) is read and enforced: `config.tov` VirtualKeyConfig, `mcp.tov` keyRuleAllows and McpManager.allToolsFor/mayRun. L184: a key without the field gets no MCP tools but those of allow_by_default clients, as in Bifrost (`gateway.tov` `keyMcp`). |
| governance.auth_config | governance.auth_config | done | `config.tov` AdminAuthConfig. |
| business_units, projects, roles | governance.* | done | Business units and projects are done, with x-bf-project-id and x-bf-customer-id (L84). Custom roles are in the management API. L97p: project members, split policies and entity_dac; L103: rate-limit shares per member and Bifrost's role-id API shape. L165: the built-in roles have fixed ids (admin 9001, editor 9002, viewer 9003, auditor 9004) and are listed first on /api/governance/rbac/roles with is_system_role (see (f) RBAC fixed roles). |
| access_profiles (governance) | not in Bifrost's governance schema (top-level access_profiles, see c1) | done (Raiju-only) | `profiles.tov` applyProfiles, validateProfile. Raiju's own shape. |
| pricing_overrides | governance.pricing_overrides | done | `store.tov` pricingRoute; config `governance.pricing_overrides` (commit 31f4aaa). |
| model_configs | governance.model_configs | done | `store.tov` modelConfigRoute (commit 31f4aaa). |
| providers (provider-level governance) | governance.providers | done | `store.tov` providerGovRoute (commit 31f4aaa). |
| complexity_analyzer_config | governance.complexity_analyzer_config | done | Raiju has top-level `complexity_router`. L165: the governance path is read into it (`config_compat.tov` mapGovernanceAnalyzer, before the routing plugin's copy, so it wins; a complexity_router in the file wins over both): the semantic classifier (Bifrost's default, on when its block is there) with provider, embedding_model, timeout, min_similarity, message_history_count and the tier keyword lists as its exemplars (the legacy code and technical lists join medium, reasoning joins complex), the decision classifier, or the llm block alone. Design (owner decision 5 after wave 22): tier_boundaries and the lexical keyword scoring are not applied; Raiju keeps its heuristic scorer on its own scale. L180: session is read into `complexity_router.session`; semantic.fallback (none, llm or decision) is copied, and the llm or decision block it names comes along as the fallback; semantic.count_toward_budgets, llm.count_toward_budgets and semantic.vector_store are copied (see (f) Complexity analyzer config). |

### c5. mcp.*

Bifrost: `config.schema.json` `mcp` and `$defs/mcp_client_config`. Raiju: `src/mcp.tov` (`McpClientConfig`, `McpConfig`), `src/mcpapi.tov` (management routes), `src/mcpauth.tov` (auth).

| Feature | Bifrost | Raiju status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| client_configs[]: name, connection_type (http, sse, stdio), connection_string, headers, stdio_config, tools_to_execute | mcp_client_config | done | `mcp.tov` McpClientConfig; `mcpapi.tov` validates connection_type. |
| connection_type inprocess, openapi | mcp_client_config.connection_type | done | openapi done: `openapi.tov` and `mcp.tov` McpSession `loadOpenApi`/`callOpenApi`. inprocess is not configurable, as Bifrost: its in-process server is a Go factory set in code (`core/schemas/mcp.go` InProcessServerFactory, `json:"-"`), so config.json cannot declare one. The OpenAPI parsing details are in openapi_config. |
| openapi_config | mcp_client_config | done | L166: `openapi.tov`, a port of `framework/openapimcp`: OpenAPI 3.0 to 3.2 and Swagger 2.0 documents, JSON or YAML (`yaml.tov`), from `spec`, `spec_file` (config.json only, inside the config folder) or `spec_url`. Local `$ref`s resolve at their point of use (3.1 sibling keywords merged, cycles cut with a placeholder, an external `$ref` makes only its operation unsupported); each operation becomes a tool named as Bifrost names them, with the parameters and the body's properties flattened into one input schema (a clash suffixed `__<in>`, readOnly properties and unknown keywords dropped, 3.1 type arrays and allOf folded); per-operation `security` (else the document's) picks the first requirement the `security_credentials` satisfy (apiKey header/query/cookie, bearer, basic; `security: []` sends none); servers and their variables, operation servers, base_url; JSON, form and text bodies; path/query/header/cookie styles; answers formatted as Bifrost's executor does (`HTTP <code>` errors, a cap with a notice, binary noted); the API description served as the server's instructions; the API checks a document on save (tools, base URL, tool lists naming real tools) and stores spec_size, spec_hash, spec_title, openapi_version and operation_count. L177: 3.2 `querystring` parameters as Bifrost's flattenQuerystring: an object schema with named properties gives one query argument per property (suffixed `__querystring` on a clash; an optional querystring keeps its fields optional and enforces its required ones once any is sent; a required field whose schema is false makes the operation unsupported, an optional querystring with one is left out with a warning), any other schema one argument sent as the raw query string (after the call's own query, the base URL's and the credentials' names dropped from it), and a JSON one percent-encoded (`openapi.tov` `flattenQuerystring`, `requestFor`). Form bodies read their encoding entries: deepObject (`field[key]`), exploded form (each member a field), packed form (key,value pairs), and space or pipe delimited and unexploded arrays (`formDelimiters`, `formObjectStyles`); a form body is encoded sorted by key as Go's url.Values. e2e: l177_e2e. L188: a tool call that carries credentials (a query, or a header besides Accept and Content-Type) gets a redirect to another host, or from https to http, back as the 3xx result (Bifrost's `Executor.clientFor`); other redirects are followed, at most ten (`redirects.tov`). |
| auth_type: none, headers, oauth | mcp_client_config.auth_type | done | `mcpauth.tov`. oauth is the authorization-code flow with PKCE, dynamic registration and `client_id` (L91). |
| auth_type: per_user_oauth, per_user_headers, token_exchange; per_user_header_keys | mcp_client_config.auth_type | done | per_user_headers done in L97k (`mcpapi.tov` perUserError, headersRouteAsync). per_user_oauth done in L97o (`mcpuser.tov`, `gateway.tov` userAuth). token_exchange configured, verified and exchanged per call (L112b, see the token exchange row), its admin credential renewed with the refresh token (L140), once across the workers and nodes (L147, see the token exchange row). Remaining token-exchange items are in (g) Auth: token exchange. |
| tools_to_auto_execute (per client) | mcp_client_config | done | `mcp.tov` (per-client list; `*` for all). Global `client.mcp_auto_execute` is Raiju-only (c2). |
| tools_to_execute | mcp_client_config | done | `mcp.tov` McpClientConfig. |
| endpoint_slug, allow_by_default, allow_on_all_virtual_keys, disabled | mcp_client_config | done | `mcpapi.tov` (CLIENT_KINDS; management API); allow_by_default from L76. |
| is_ping_available, needs_session_stickiness | mcp_client_config | done | Stored and returned (`mcpapi.tov` CLIENT_KINDS). needs_session_stickiness false makes an http client connect fresh before each call (`mcp.tov` McpSession.callTool); new http clients default to false, as Bifrost. L166: Bifrost's connection checker (`mcp.tov` McpManager.startToolSync, McpSession.check): every enabled client is checked every tool_sync_interval (its own, else the global one, else 10 minutes): a sticky client is pinged (MCP `ping`, unless is_ping_available is false) and its tools listed, and a failure reconnects it and checks again after 10 s; a per-call client lists its tools and re-reads its instructions; a client that is not connected reconnects. |
| require_public_target (SSRF guard) | mcp_client_config | done | `netguard.tov` (private and metadata hosts, shared with webhooks' `urlProblem`), checked in `mcp.tov` McpSession.send, postAs, the spec fetch and callOpenApi. L186: hosts are parsed as the resolver reads them (inet_aton IPv4 forms, IPv6 with embedded IPv4, trailing dots) and a host name is resolved before each request, every address checked (`resolvedTargetProblem`), as Bifrost's SSRF-safe dialer does; the OAuth metadata discovery of an MCP client is checked the same way whatever require_public_target says (Bifrost's SSRF-hardened discovery client). The allow list is `mcp.oauth_private_allowlist` (hosts, addresses and CIDR ranges, as Bifrost's `NewAllowlist`). A client registered over the API while dashboard auth is off gets it set (`mcpapi.tov` createClient, `server.tov` passes `!ops.authOn`). L188: with require_public_target, the runtime checks the addresses again when each connection is made (fetch's `peer` option, `tov-patches/0013-fetch-peer-policy.patch`), so a DNS answer that changes after the check (rebinding) is refused too (the MCP connection, the OpenAPI spec and every tool call, `mcp.tov` McpSession.peer); the OAuth metadata discovery is checked the same way whatever require_public_target says. An OpenAPI tool call follows redirects (at most ten), but one that carries credentials gets a redirect to another host, or from https to http, back as the 3xx, as Bifrost's `Executor.clientFor` does (`redirects.tov` `credentialRule`, `openapi.tov` callOperation). |
| tls_config | mcp_client_config | done | `mcp.tov` McpTlsConfig. |
| allowed_extra_headers | mcp_client_config | done | `mcp.tov` McpSession.forwardable: the request headers the allow list names (`*` for all) reach the server on a tool call; `gateway.tov` toolGate and `ops.tov` `mcp` pass the request's headers into McpManager.execute. Framing headers are never forwarded. |
| tool_pricing | mcp_client_config | done | `mcp.tov` McpManager.priceOf; `gateway.tov` chargeTool debits the caller's budgets (`governance.tov` record) after each call, and a tool call is refused with 402 when those budgets are used up (`governance.tov` admitTool). L166: the cost is on the MCP log entry (`mcp.tov` McpToolCall.cost, `ops.tov` recordMcp), and admitTool charges what funds the caller as inference does: the project the request names (with or without a key, by its accounting_mode, with the member's slice), and for a key its own budget, its team's, business unit's and reached customers' (a team's customer included; x-bf-customer-id narrows them) and its user's held profiles. |
| tool_execution_timeout (per client) | mcp_client_config | done | `mcp.tov` `durationMsOf` (Go durations, or a bare number of seconds); McpSession's constructor uses it over the global `mcp.tool_execution_timeout_seconds`. Integers are turned into duration strings in `config_compat.tov` clientItems. |
| tool_sync_interval (per client) | mcp_client_config | done | `mcp.tov` refresh timers, one per client (G22, L76). |
| is_code_mode_client | mcp_client_config | done | L117: the client's tools are reached through the code mode meta-tools (`src/codemode.tov`), not offered one by one; the management API takes the flag. |
| max_instructions_length | mcp_client_config | done | L134. The server's initialize `instructions` are read at connect (`mcp.tov` McpSession.instructions) and forwarded as Bifrost does (`mcpinstructions.tov`, a port of core/mcp/instructions.go): one `<mcp_server name="...">` block per server the caller can reach (a server with no visible tool is left out), sorted by name, a forged block tag in the text neutralised, each cut on a character boundary to the client's max_instructions_length (else mcp.tool_manager_config.max_instructions_per_client, 4096) with a truncation notice, the whole set cut to max_instructions_total (16384) with a closing line naming the servers left out. They go to the model as a system message ahead of the caller's own on chat requests that carry MCP tools (`gateway.tov` withMcpInstructions, scoped by the key's rules, x-bf-mcp-include-clients and x-bf-mcp-include-tools), in the gateway's own `/mcp` initialize answer (`mcpserver.tov`, `ops.tov` mcp), and as comments in a code mode client's stub files and docs (`codemode.tov`). The management API takes the field (negative is a 400). L166: Responses requests get the MCP tools in the Responses shape and the instructions appended to their own `instructions` (Bifrost's ParseAndAddToolsToRequest; `gateway.tov` withMcpResponses, `native.tov` responsesApi), and a per-call client (http without session stickiness, per-user and token exchange auth) re-reads its instructions at each periodic check and with each fresh handshake (`mcp.tov` McpSession.check, callTool), as Bifrost's per-call discovery does; a sticky client re-reads them on reconnect and refresh-tools. |
| mcp.tool_groups | mcp.tool_groups | done | Bifrost's OSS schema calls these enterprise and stores them as virtual MCPs. `config_compat.tov` mapMcp reads each group as a virtual MCP, and its team_ids, customer_ids and provider_names reach the virtual keys they name (groupKeyIds). user_ids and api_key_ids are noted as not applied (Raiju's keys have no user or API key ids). |
| mcp.tool_manager_config | mcp.tool_manager_config | done | `config_compat.tov` mapToolManager: tool_execution_timeout, oauth_private_allowlist, max_agent_depth and disable_auto_tool_inject are applied, and code_mode_binding_level and code_mode_limits (L117). max_instructions_per_client and max_instructions_total are applied (L134, `mcpinstructions.tov`). |
| mcp.virtual_mcps | mcp.virtual_mcps | done | `config_compat.tov` mapMcp; read as top-level `virtual_mcps` (commit 0c999ff). |
| mcp.tool_sync_interval (global) | mcp.tool_sync_interval | done | `mcp.tov` McpManager.startToolSync: a client's own interval, else this default (`config_compat.tov` also maps the legacy client.mcp_tool_sync_interval). Deviation: with neither set there is no periodic re-list; Bifrost falls back to 10 minutes. |

## (d) Providers

### d1. Provider list

Bifrost: provider constants `core/schemas/bifrost.go:49-82`, and per-provider pages in `docs/providers/supported-providers/`. Raiju: `src/providers/openai.tov` (`makeEndpoints`), `longtail.tov` (`longtailTable`), `index.tov` (`supportsChat`, `supportsEmbeddings`, `supportsDecisions`, `CHAT_ONLY`), `anthropic.tov`, `gemini.tov`, `bedrock.tov`, `bedrock_mantle.tov`, `vertex.tov`, `cohere.tov`, `watsonx.tov`, `typesafe.tov`, `cloudflare.tov`, `databricks.tov`, `githubcopilot.tov`, `replicate.tov`. Routing of files, batches, images, speech and transcription is in `src/media.tov` (`matchRoute`, `providerTarget`, `nativeMedia`, `supported`), `media_native.tov` (Anthropic, Gemini), and `media_cloud.tov` (Vertex, Bedrock).

The notes list only the operations that differ from the "Supported Operations" table in the Bifrost docs. Where a row says a media route is "passed through", the OpenAI-compatible media routes send the request to the provider's own endpoint for any OpenAI-format provider (`media.tov:supported`); that is not checked against each provider's API.

| Feature | Bifrost | Raiju status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| openai | supported-providers/openai.mdx: chat, responses, responses lifecycle, text, embeddings, decisions, speech, STT, images, image edit, files, batch, videos, compaction, models | done | Raiju: chat, streaming, responses (lifecycle, compaction, input_tokens), text, embeddings, speech, STT, images (generate, edit, variation), files, batch, realtime, models (`openai.tov:makeEndpoints`, `media.tov:matchRoute`). Decisions: gpt-6-luna natively on `/v1/decisions` (L163, `providers/decision_native.tov` openaiBody, openaiDecision); another model is refused, or emulated under `client.compat.decisions`, as Bifrost does. Videos out-of-scope. |
| azure | supported-providers/azure.mdx: chat, responses, embeddings, files, images, image edit, models, compaction, decisions, batch | done | Raiju serves Azure through `openai.tov` and `media.tov` (deployment routes: `deploymentRoute`). Claude deployments go to the resource's /anthropic/v1/messages (L181, `providers/anthropic_route.tov`; see (d2) Model aliases). Entra ID is done (commit 6aeb7f2). Decisions natively on the Foundry `/providers/microsoft/v1/systemone` with the systemone body (L163, `providers/decision_native.tov`). |
| anthropic | supported-providers/anthropic.mdx: chat, responses, text, files, batch, models | done | Raiju: chat, streaming, responses (bridged), count tokens (exact, `extras.tov:countTokens`), models (`server.tov:upstreamModels`), files and batches (`media_native.tov`, routed by `media.tov:nativeMedia`). Legacy text completions on `/v1/complete` (L163, `providers/anthropic.tov` textRequest; not streamed, as in Bifrost). |
| bedrock | supported-providers/bedrock.mdx: chat, responses, text, embeddings, files, batch, models, images, image edit and variation, count tokens | done | Raiju: Converse chat and streaming, responses (bridged), embeddings (Titan and Cohere), role_arn, count tokens (exact through Bedrock CountTokens, `extras.tov:bedrockCountTokens`; an estimate where Bedrock cannot count the model), rerank (`extras.tov:rerankText`), files and batches over S3 (`media_cloud.tov:s3Object`, `media.tov:nativeMedia`), image generation (Titan and Stability, `media_cloud.tov:bedrockImage`). Model list: foundation models and inference profiles, SigV4 signed on the control plane (`server.tov:nativeModelIds`, `providers/bedrock.tov:listRequest`; L97h). L97n: every page follows nextToken (`server.tov` bedrockPages, capped at 50 pages), and the listing GET is signed as a GET (`providers/bedrock.tov` signRequest; L97h had signed it as a POST). L97q: Stability edits (`media_cloud.tov` `stabilityImageEdit`). L105: a Stability generation's extra params (the fields Bifrost does not read) go into its flat body as sent (`media_cloud.tov` stabilityBody). Image edits for Titan and Nova (`media_cloud.tov` bedrockImageEdit: inpainting, outpainting, background removal) and variations for Titan and Nova (`media_cloud.tov` bedrockImageVariation); Stability variations stay refused (owner decision 6, HANDOFF.md). Legacy text on InvokeModel for Claude and Mistral models (L163, `providers/bedrock.tov` textRequest), streamed on InvokeModelWithResponseStream (L173, textStreamRequest). |
| bedrock_mantle | supported-providers/bedrock-mantle.mdx: chat (+stream), responses, tool calling, vision, reasoning, structured outputs, prompt caching, models, count tokens | done | Chat through the Bedrock OpenAI-compatible endpoint, and Claude models through Mantle's native Messages path (`bedrock_mantle.tov`, commit 6aeb7f2). L164: the model listing (GET /v1/models on the key's regional host, bearer or SigV4 for bedrock-mantle; `bedrock_mantle.tov` modelsRequest, `server.tov` ownListingIds); exact token counts for Claude models on /anthropic/v1/messages/count_tokens, from /v1/count_tokens and /anthropic/v1/messages/count_tokens (`bedrock_mantle.tov` countRequest, `extras.tov` countTokens), as Bifrost counts them (other models have no count path there and get the estimate); the provider's `cache_control_injection_points` on Claude models (`messagesText`). L173: Responses requests for the OpenAI-compatible models go to Mantle's own `/{v1,openai/v1}/responses` with the bare model, streamed as they come, as Bifrost's Responses sends them (its datasheet check falls back to the endpoint); Claude models answer through the Messages surface (`bedrock_mantle.tov` servesResponses, responsesRequest; `native.tov` responsesApi); `prompt_cache.auto_inject` (and x-bf-prompt-cache-auto-inject) marks Claude's prompt (`messagesText`); the key's `project_id` (and an alias's) is sent as OpenAI-Project on the OpenAI-compatible surface and the listing, anthropic-workspace-id on the Messages surface (`setProject`); a key with role_arn lists with the role's STS credentials (`server.tov` ownListingIds). |
| cohere | supported-providers/cohere.mdx: chat, responses, embeddings, models | done | Raiju: chat, streaming, embeddings, native rerank (`extras.tov:rerankText`), responses (bridged). Cohere SDK routes are in (a2). L164: tool_choice maps to Cohere's (none to NONE; required, any and a named function to REQUIRED, as Bifrost; auto is left out, Cohere's default, where Bifrost sends REQUIRED for it) (`cohere.tov` toolChoice); the model listing (/v1/models) and exact token counts through /v1/tokenize. |
| vertex | supported-providers/vertex.mdx: chat, responses, embeddings, images, image edit, video (out), models, text, batch and files through genai routes | done | Raiju: chat, streaming, embeddings, responses, images (Imagen and Gemini image models, `media_cloud.tov:vertexImage`), batches and files over GCS (`media_cloud.tov:vertexJobCreate`, `gcsUpload`). Count tokens are exact for Claude (count-tokens:rawPredict) and Gemini (:countTokens) (`providers/vertex.tov:countRequest`, `extras.tov:countTokens`; L97h). Model list: the google, anthropic and mistralai publishers, every page (`server.tov:nativeModelIds`; L97h). |
| mistral | supported-providers/mistral.mdx: chat, responses, STT, OCR, embeddings, models | done | Raiju: OpenAI-compatible (`openai.tov:makeEndpoints`); STT and OCR routed through `media.tov`. |
| ollama | supported-providers/ollama.mdx: chat, responses, text, embeddings, models, decisions | done | Raiju: `openai.tov:makeEndpoints`. Decisions natively on `/v1/systemone` (L163, `providers/decision_native.tov` ollamaBody: input images as raw base64 in `images`, with the native `images` and `keep_alive` lifted from the extra params). |
| opencode-go | supported-providers/opencode.mdx: chat, responses, models | done | OpenAI-compatible base (`longtail.tov:longtailTable`). |
| opencode-zen | supported-providers/opencode.mdx: chat, responses, models | done | OpenAI-compatible base (`longtail.tov:longtailTable`). |

| gemini | supported-providers/gemini.mdx: chat, responses, speech, STT, images, image edit, video (out), embeddings, files, batch, models, count tokens | done | Raiju: chat, streaming, responses, embeddings, count tokens (exact), models (`server.tov:upstreamModels`), speech (`media_native.tov:geminiSpeech`), STT (`media_native.tov:geminiTranscription`, audio sent inline; streamed with stream=true, L181), images (`media_native.tov:geminiImageGeneration`, Imagen and Gemini image), files and batches (`media_native.tov`). tool_choice maps to toolConfig (none, auto, required/any, a named function as allowedFunctionNames), only when function declarations exist, as Bifrost (L164: `gemini.tov` toolConfig). Video out-of-scope. |
| github-copilot | supported-providers/github-copilot.mdx: chat, responses, models | done | Chat through a Copilot token or a GitHub App (`githubcopilot.tov`). L164: the model listing (GET <host>/models with the bearer token and the editor headers; a GitHub App key's token and host minted and cached as for chat; `githubcopilot.tov` modelsRequest, `server.tov` ownListingIds), and Responses through the chat bridge (`native.tov` responsesApi), as Bifrost converts them through chat. Embeddings and the other operations are refused, as Bifrost refuses them (`CHAT_ONLY` in `index.tov`). |
| huggingface | supported-providers/huggingface.mdx: routes to 19 inference backends (fal-ai, hf-inference, cerebras, sambanova and others) with chat, embeddings, speech, transcription and image operations | done | L194 (`providers/huggingface.tov`, Bifrost's core/providers/huggingface): a model is `huggingface/<inference provider>/<hub id>` (one slash: a hub id with no provider, Bifrost's splitIntoModelProvider). Chat (and Responses through chat, streamed or not) goes to the router's /v1/chat/completions as `<hub id>:<provider>` (`openai.tov` chatRequest). Embeddings, speech, transcription and image generation read the hub's inferenceProviderMapping (GET huggingface.co/api/models/<id>?expand[]=inferenceProviderMapping, no credentials, cached per model; `media.tov` hfMappings, and `gateway.tov` fetches it before an embeddings build through hfEnsure) and refuse a provider that does not map the model with the operation's task (feature-extraction, text-to-speech, automatic-speech-recognition, text-to-image) as Bifrost's unsupported operation; a hub refusal keeps its status and message. Each provider's route on the router (Bifrost's getInferenceProviderRouteURL: /hf-inference/models/<id>[/pipeline/feature-extraction or text-to-speech], /fal-ai/<id>, /nebius/v1/embeddings and images/generations, /together/v1/images/generations, /sambanova and /scaleway /v1/embeddings, /replicate/v1/prediction) and body: embeddings `inputs` for hf-inference, `input` with provider and model for the others, normalize, prompt_name, truncate, truncation_direction, encoding_format and dimensions, the mapped id as model; answers as an OpenAI object or a 1-D or 2-D array become OpenAI's list (`embeddingsBody`, `embeddingsResponse`). Speech: {text, provider, model, parameters.generation_parameters}, the answer's audio URL downloaded (`media.tov` hfMedia). Transcription: hf-inference takes the raw audio with its detected MIME type, fal-ai a base64 data URI in audio_url (WAV refused, Bifrost's message), the others base64 inputs with provider and model; text and chunks come back as text and segments (response_format text answers the text). Images: hf-inference {inputs, model} answered with raw bytes (as b64_json), fal-ai's options (num_images, image_size from size, sync_mode for b64_json, jpg as jpeg, the safety checker off for moderation low, guidance_scale, acceleration, enable_prompt_expansion), nebius's (width and height, response_extension, loras) and together's (base64, steps); fal-ai, nebius and together take the model as named without a hub lookup. fal-ai streams images and edits (stream: POST /fal-ai/<id>/stream, each image an `image_generation.partial_image` or `image_edit.partial_image` event, the last again as `.completed`, an error event ends it; `FalStream`). Image edits are fal-ai's only (image_url for single-image models, image_urls for multi-image ones or several images, use_image_urls overriding). A 404 from a provider route clears the model's cached mapping and the call is retried once with a fresh one (media routes). Models listing: each inference provider's hub listing (200 most liked, with the key), models whose pipeline tag or tags serve something listed as huggingface/<provider>/<id>, a failing provider left out (`server.tov` nativeModelIds). `RAIJU_HF_HUB_URL` points the hub reads at a mirror. Differences: the provider's own model id is used in the route from the first try (Bifrost first tries the hub id, then retries after a 404); embeddings retry no 404 (the next call refetches the mapping only when a media call cleared it); the 2 MB request limit is the router's to enforce; the listing reads providers one after another. e2e `l194_e2e`. |

The mask of an edit is not sent, as Bifrost does not send it (replicate/images.go ToReplicateImageEditInput). L173: the models listing reads the deployments only with `replicate_key_config.use_deployments_endpoint`; without it the listing is empty and the listing pipeline adds the key's aliases, as Bifrost lists it (`server.tov` nativeModelIds); a chat request's extra params (its top-level fields that are not chat fields) are merged into the prediction input, the caller's value replacing a field Raiju set, and webhook and webhook_events_filter also go on the prediction, as Bifrost's ToReplicateChatRequest; presence_penalty, frequency_penalty, seed and reasoning_effort now carry over (`replicate.tov` chatBody, extraParams). Variations: Bifrost has none. Video: out of scope. |

### d2. Operations across providers

Bifrost: `core/schemas/bifrost.go:135-200` (request types), `core/providers/<name>/` (implementations). Raiju: `src/providers/index.tov` (chat and embeddings dispatch), `src/extras.tov` (rerank, count tokens, decisions), `src/media.tov` and its cloud and native modules (files, batches, images, speech, transcription).

| Feature | Bifrost | Raiju status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chat completions (non-stream) | core/providers | done | `index.tov:supportsChat` and the chat dispatch in `index.tov`. |
| Chat completions (stream) | core/providers | done | `index.tov` dispatch; `server.tov` and `front.tov`. Native SSE for OpenAI-format and Azure; converted for Anthropic, Gemini, Bedrock, Cohere, Vertex (`streams.tov`, `streams_cohere.tov`). |
| Text completions | ChatCompletion-style text (text_completion) | done | OpenAI-format providers (`gateway.tov`, text completion route), Anthropic's legacy `/v1/complete` and Bedrock InvokeModel for Claude and Mistral models (L163, `providers/anthropic.tov` and `providers/bedrock.tov` textRequest), streamed on Bedrock's InvokeModelWithResponseStream (L173, `providers/bedrock.tov` textStreamRequest, `streams.tov` invokeText). Anthropic's text stream is refused, as Bifrost has none. |
| Responses API (create) | responses | done | Native OpenAI, translated others (`native.tov`). |
| Responses lifecycle (retrieve, delete, cancel, input_items) | responses_retrieve, responses_delete, responses_cancel, responses_input_items | done | `media.tov:matchRoute` (commit 12757de). OpenAI and Azure. |
| Responses input_tokens and compaction | responses_* | done | `media.tov:matchRoute` (commit 12757de). |
| Responses over WebSocket (GET /v1/responses upgrade) | responses (wsresponses.go) | done | `wsresponses.tov:ResponsesSocket`: each response.create runs the responses path and streams its events as they arrive (L102). response.cancel is a Raiju extension. |
| Live sessions (WebSocket and WebRTC create) | livesession.go, livecontrol.go, wslive.go, webrtclive.go | done | `live.tov:LiveSocket`: GPT Live over WebSocket, OpenAI only, as Bifrost (no other provider has a GPT Live API there); attach and content restricted to the owning virtual key. POST /v1/live/sessions (WebRTC create) is terminated by a gateway peer on each side and every data-channel event is metered, guarded and logged (L122): voice seconds from `session.usage.updated` and `session.closed` in 30 s windows (each window admitted again by governance, a refusal ends the session with an error event and a `session.close` upstream), the 15 s minimum a created WebRTC session bills, token usage of delegated `response.event`s, the transcript, input guards on session.update, instructions and item text, output guards on transcript and text deltas, the server-event permissions of the create body (`allowed_server_events`, widened for the provider and kept for the browser), draining until `session.closed` (15 s) and a browser that leaves. Each billing unit is a log row (L137, as Bifrost's live meter closes them: a window of 30 s of voice time, each delegated response with its model's tokens, and the unit that ends the session, which carries the rest of the voice time up to the 15 s minimum, the transcript as input and output, the status and `live_close_reason`; `op` live, with `live_unit`, `live_unit_index`, `live_audio_seconds`, `live_billed_seconds` and `live_session_id` in the metadata and the session as `parent_request_id`), each unit is charged to the budgets and token limits when it closes, the next one having been admitted first, and `raiju_live_webrtc_sessions`, `raiju_live_webrtc_events_total` and `raiju_live_audio_seconds_total` count them. The WebSocket path (`live.tov` `LiveSocket`) runs a primary session through the same `LiveCore` (L128): voice seconds in 30 s windows that governance admits again (no 15 s minimum: only a created WebRTC session bills one), guards on both sides' text, the transcript, the key binding of the provider's session id, the session as one row (`live_transport` websocket) and `raiju_live_ws_sessions`, `raiju_live_ws_events_total` and `raiju_live_audio_seconds_total`. An attach (sideband) bills nothing and relays frames untouched, as in Bifrost. Cost (L128, `livehooks.tov` `liveCost`): the billed voice seconds at the model's `input_cost_per_second` plus the tokens of delegated responses at the text rates of the model that ran them (`response.model`, else `session.delegation.responses.model`), as Bifrost's `computeLiveCost` and Responses usage; a model with no such rate costs 0 for its seconds. Delegation (L169, `live.tov` `checkDelegation`, as Bifrost's `liveBackendModel` and `liveUpdatePolicy.check`): a start or WebRTC create that delegates to Responses must name `session.delegation.responses.model`, served by the session's provider (else 400 "must be served by openai, the provider of session.model") and by the session's key, and governance admits it; it goes upstream bare (prefix stripped, the key's alias resolved). Each `session.update` naming one is checked the same way before it reaches the provider: a refusal is an error event and the frame is dropped (the session goes on); an admitted one switches the model delegated responses are billed under. An attach's `session.update` gets the provider and key checks, as Bifrost's sideband relay. The governance check takes no request of its own (`governance.tov` `admit` peek): each delegated response is admitted and charged as it closes, as before. |
| Embeddings | embedding | done | `index.tov:supportsEmbeddings` and `embeddingsUpstream`. Anthropic has no embeddings upstream in Bifrost either. |
| Speech (TTS) | speech, speech_stream | done | OpenAI-compatible (pass-through), Azure and Gemini (`media_native.tov:geminiSpeech`). A streamed answer (`stream_format` sse) passes through as its events arrive (L169, `media.tov` `send`, `pump`). Sarvam natively (L164, Bifrost's sarvam provider: `media_native.tov` `sarvamSpeech`, `media.tov` `sarvamMedia`; its streamed speech L169): `/text-to-speech` with Bifrost's body (input as text, `language_code` or `target_language_code`, voice or `speaker`, speed or `pace`, Sarvam's own fields), its first base64 audio decoded as the answer (typed by `output_audio_codec`, WAV by default), and with `stream_format` sse `/text-to-speech/stream`, whose raw audio goes to the caller as it comes, each chunk a `speech.audio.delta` event, then `speech.audio.done` and `[DONE]`; Sarvam's errors keep their status and message. Vertex has no speech in Bifrost (`vertex.go` Speech is unsupported), nor do Bedrock, Anthropic, Cohere and the other OpenAI-compatible providers but Groq and OpenRouter (served by pass-through). Billed by input characters, as Bifrost bills them (L144: `pricing.tov` `speechCost`; the answer carries no usage, so no output tokens are billed). L194: Hugging Face's text-to-speech through its inference providers (fal-ai, hf-inference, replicate), the audio downloaded from the answer's URL, as Bifrost's (see huggingface in (d1)). ElevenLabs is out of scope (media). |
| Transcription (STT) | transcription, transcription_stream | done | OpenAI-compatible (pass-through), Azure and Gemini (`media_native.tov:geminiTranscription`). A streamed transcription (`stream=true`) passes through as its events arrive, on the large-upload path too, and is billed when it ends from the usage of its last event (L169, `media.tov` `send`, `pump`, `StreamUsage`; `gateway.tov` `mediaAnswer`). Sarvam natively (L164, `media_native.tov` `sarvamTranscription`, `sarvamTranscriptToOpenAI`): `/speech-to-text` with the file, model, `mode`, `language_code` (or the language) and `input_audio_codec`, answered in Bifrost's transcription shape (text, language, words, diarized segments, the latest end as the duration, which bills its seconds; `response_format` text answers the text alone). Billed by the usage its answer reports, whole or as the last event of a stream, else the verbose `duration` (L144: `pricing.tov` `transcriptionCost`, `media.tov` `transcriptionUsage`). Gemini's `usageMetadata` is read as Bifrost reads it (`convertGeminiUsageMetadataToTranscriptionUsage`: prompt and candidate tokens, the total, the prompt's TEXT and AUDIO token counts; `media_native.tov` `transcriptionUsageOf`), returned as the JSON answer's `usage` and billed by `transcriptionCost` (audio tokens at the audio rate, text tokens at the input rate, output tokens at the output rate); a plain-text answer is billed from the same usage, kept on `MediaBill.usage` (L162). L181: Gemini's streamed transcription (`stream=true`, Bifrost's Gemini TranscriptionStream; `media_native.tov` geminiTranscriptionStream, GeminiTranscriptStream; `media.tov` geminiTranscriptStream): the same body to streamGenerateContent?alt=sse, each chunk's candidate text a transcript.text.delta event as it arrives, then transcript.text.done with the whole text and the token usage of the last chunk that reported one (type tokens: prompt, candidate and total counts, as extractGeminiUsageMetadata reads them; billed from it when the stream ends) and [DONE]; an error chunk ends the stream with an error event and no [DONE]; a refusal before the stream is Gemini's answer; e2e `l181_e2e`. L194: Hugging Face's speech-to-text through its inference providers (hf-inference raw audio, fal-ai's data URI without WAV, the others' base64 inputs), chunks as segments (see huggingface in (d1)). ElevenLabs is out of scope (media). |
| Images (generate, edit, variation) | image_generation, image_edit, image_variation | done | Generation: OpenAI-compatible, Azure, Gemini (Imagen and Gemini image), Vertex (`media_cloud.tov:vertexImage`), Bedrock (`media_cloud.tov:bedrockImage`). Edits: OpenAI-compatible and Azure, Gemini (`media_native.tov` geminiImageEdit), Vertex (`media_cloud.tov` vertexImageEdit), Bedrock Titan, Nova and Stability (`media_cloud.tov` bedrockImageEdit, stabilityImageEdit) and Replicate (`replicate.tov` imageEditRequest). Variations: OpenAI-compatible, Azure, and Bedrock Titan and Nova (`media_cloud.tov` bedrockImageVariation); Design (owner decision 6, 2026-10-17): Bedrock Stability variations stay refused. L174: Stability's other form options pass through on edits (see (a1) POST /v1/images/edits); the Replicate edit mask is not sent, as Bifrost's ToReplicateImageEditInput does not send it either. L194: Hugging Face's image backends (hf-inference, fal-ai with streaming, nebius, together; fal-ai edits and edit streams; see huggingface in (d1)), and Replicate's input as Bifrost's ToReplicateImageGenerationInput and ToReplicateImageEditInput build it (`replicate.tov` predictionInput): n as number_of_images (was num_outputs), size as aspect_ratio and resolution (1K, 2K, 4K; an explicit aspect_ratio, and an extra resolution, win), output_format, quality, background, seed, negative_prompt, num_inference_steps, input_images in the model's image field, the input's keys sorted as Bifrost marshals them, the request's other fields merged in as extra params (an edit form's other text fields too) and webhook and webhook_events_filter also on the prediction. Bedrock Stability variations stay refused (owner decision 6). |
| Files | file_upload, file_list, file_retrieve, file_delete, file_content | done | OpenAI-compatible, Azure, Anthropic, Gemini, Vertex (GCS) and Bedrock (S3) (`media.tov:nativeMedia`, `media_cloud.tov`). Wafer and Replicate uploads too (d1 rows). Cohere has no files, as in Bifrost (its Cohere File* calls are unsupported, `core/providers/cohere/cohere.go:1263-1285`). |
| Batches | batch_create, batch_list, batch_retrieve, batch_cancel, batch_results, batch_delete | done | OpenAI-compatible, Azure, Anthropic, Gemini, Vertex and Bedrock. The results route is served (`media.tov:matchRoute`, `batches/{id}/results`). Cohere batches are refused with a 400 (`media.tov` supported), as Bifrost refuses them (core/providers/cohere/cohere.go BatchCreate and the other batch methods return NewUnsupportedOperationError). batch_delete (L164), where Bifrost serves it: the GenAI route DELETE /genai/v1beta/batches/{id} passes to Gemini, and for Vertex (x-model-provider) deletes the job and answers {"done":true}, as Bifrost's Vertex BatchDelete (`native.tov` genaiStore, `media.tov` vertexBatchDelete); DELETE on Vertex's regional batchPredictionJobs route passes through; other providers refuse it, as Bifrost refuses it. |
| Rerank | (provider rerank) | done | Cohere, vLLM and Bedrock natively (`extras.tov:rerankText`). `raiju/lexical` is a local lexical ranking (`extras.tov`). Others error. |
| Count tokens | count_tokens | done | Exact for Anthropic, Gemini and Vertex (`extras.tov:countTokens`, `providers/vertex.tov:countRequest`) and for Bedrock models Bedrock can count (`extras.tov:bedrockCountTokens`). L164, as Bifrost counts them: OpenAI (and custom providers on its base) through /v1/responses/input_tokens, the chat body sent as a Responses body (`openai.tov` inputTokensRequest); Claude models on Bedrock Mantle (`bedrock_mantle.tov` countRequest); vLLM and SGLang on /v1/messages/count_tokens and DeepSeek on /anthropic/v1/messages/count_tokens (`openai.tov` messagesCountRequest); Cohere through its tokenizer, /v1/tokenize with the conversation's text (`cohere.tov` tokenizeRequest). POST /v1/responses/input_tokens with another provider's model is counted by that provider's count (`extras.tov` responsesInputTokens; Bifrost's count response shape, `object` response.input_tokens). Estimated elsewhere, marked `"exact": false` (Bifrost refuses those providers). L174: /anthropic/v1/messages/count_tokens with another provider's model converts the Messages body to chat and counts it as /v1/count_tokens does (exactly where that provider counts), answered in Anthropic's shape `{"input_tokens"}` (`extras.tov` countTokensOf), as Bifrost's route converts the request for every provider; e2e `l174_e2e`. Difference: where Bifrost refuses, Raiju answers its estimate. G33. |

Transcription sessions are billed per transcription at the transcription rates, minted client secrets connect as their virtual key, and failed or cancelled responses are logged with their status (L160). A `response.create` while a response is running (from a `response.create`, or the provider's `input_audio_buffer.committed`, until its `response.done`) is refused with Bifrost's error event ("Conversation already has an active response in progress.", `invalid_request_error`) and never reaches the provider; the session goes on (L169, `webrtc.tov` `LiveCore.fromClient`). A call refuses a second `response.create` while a response runs, as the WebSocket does (L169). Providers without credentials are skipped; `tests/test_smoke_config.py` (in `make unit`) checks the config builder. Run here with no provider keys only (the AWS variables present were rejected by AWS), and it found a use-after-free in Tov patch 0008's `httpBodyCancel` that crashed the gateway when a large request's provider could not be reached (fixed in the patch; e2e `l157_e2e`). Not covered: Bifrost's wider scenario matrix (images, audio, files, batches, reasoning, structured outputs, multi-turn tool loops), the SDK integration suites, and the long-tail OpenAI-compatible providers. |

## (e) Plugins

Bifrost: `plugins/<name>/main.go` (plugin names at `PluginName` constants). Raiju: plugin chain and built-in plugins in `src/plugins.tov` (`makePlugin`, `buildChain`); Bifrost plugin config is mapped by `src/config_compat.tov` (`mapPlugins`); feature code in `src/`.

| Feature | Bifrost | Raiju status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| governance plugin | plugins/governance (store.go, resolver.go, tracker.go, mcp.go) | done (built in) | `governance.tov`, `profiles.tov`, `rbac.tov`. Built in rather than loaded as a plugin; its config maps onto the client section. |
| logging plugin | plugins/logging (writer.go, costrecalc.go, live.go) | done | `logstore.tov` (JSONL files, in-memory index, analytics, MCP logs), `gateway.tov` (log writes). The live feed is the `/ws` UI socket (`ops.tov`, `ws.tov`), scoped by data access. Cost recalculation is in (L97l). Differs internally: JSONL files with an in-memory index instead of a SQL store, behind the same log routes; queries across workers are open in (h2) Request log store. The /ws registry is shared across workers (L102). |
| telemetry plugin (Prometheus) | plugins/telemetry/main.go | done | Families and labels follow Bifrost (`metrics.tov`). Enrichment labels are recorded. The routing-classifier families and the push gateway are done (L97i, h1). L97m: client.prometheus_labels reach the http_* families (`observeHttp`, `httpCustomValues`); telemetry.overhead_breakdown_enabled (default off, as Bifrost) gates bifrost_overhead_component_microseconds; telemetry.user_labels_enabled adds user_id and user_name to the bifrost_* families. L170: user_name is the governance user's name for the request's user id (`gateway.tov` `userNameOf`, from `governance.users`), on chat, the other routes and the media billing rows. |
| otel plugin | plugins/otel (traces and metrics, gRPC or HTTP, span filters) | done | Traces and metrics over OTLP HTTP and OTLP gRPC (plaintext h2c; TLS through the ALPN patch, L92) (`otel.tov`, `otel_metrics.tov`, `grpc.tov`). Plugin options `excluded_attributes`, `request_headers`, `disable_content_logging`, `plugin_span_filter`, `export_overhead_spans`, `group_traces_by_session`, `disable_root_span_content` and `overhead_breakdown_enabled` are applied (L97m). `trace_type` is accepted and ignored, as Bifrost ignores it. Raiju emits plugin spans for governance, semantic_cache and routing, and overhead spans (convertor), with their real start times. Raiju puts no message content on spans, so disable_root_span_content has nothing to drop today. |
| maxim plugin | plugins/maxim | partial | Mapped to the Maxim log sink (`sink_maxim.tov`, L89; `config_compat.tov` maps `plugins[maxim]`). Traces and generations are pushed to the SDK log endpoint. L192: the sink writes the trace the plugin's hooks write, in maxim-go's commit log lines (`<entity>{id=...,action=...,data=...}`, read from maxim-go v0.2.1's CommitLog.Serialize; the earlier envelope was inferred): trace create (name bifrost_<request type> or x-bf-maxim-trace-name, sessionId, startTimestamp), update {input: the latest message}, add-generation (provider, model, messages and modelParameters from the request body, x-bf-maxim-generation-name), the generation's result (the response) or error ({message, code, type}) and end, the trace's end, and one tag update per tag on the generation and the trace: the x-bf-maxim-* tags, the row's dimensions and logging headers not set by them, model, upstream_latency_ms and overhead_latency_ms, and header.<name> for the request headers the exporter's request_headers names (credential headers REDACTED; `plugins[maxim].config.request_headers` maps to it). An x-bf-maxim-generation-id gets only its result (the trace is ended only when x-bf-maxim-trace-id names it), and realtime turns, MCP and A2A rows are not traced, as in the plugin. Differs: the trace is written from the log row after the request, so its messages and result need the row's content (logs content logging, and include_content, which a Bifrost maxim plugin maps on unless disable_content_logging), and the trace and generation ids default to the request id and the row id rather than new UUIDs. Open: attachments (image, file and audio parts, which the plugin uploads through maxim-go's attachment API) are not sent. Not in `SCOPE.md`. |
| mocker plugin | plugins/mocker/main.go | done | `plugins.tov:MockerPlugin` (L97i): Bifrost's rule shape: `name`, `enabled`, `priority` (higher first), `conditions` (`providers`, `models`, `message_regex` through `regex.tov`, `request_size`), `probability`, weighted `responses` (success with `message`, `message_template`, `model`, `usage`, `finish_reason`; error with `status_code`, `type`, `code`), `latency` and `global_latency` (Go duration strings, fixed or uniform), and `default_behavior` (passthrough, error, success). Raiju's own `model`, `contains`, `status`, `response`, `content` and `latency_ms` still work. L170: the `{{faker.*}}` template variables (Bifrost's methods: names, email, phone, address parts, company, lorem words and sentences, uuid, numbers, booleans, dates; `plugins.tov` `fakerValue`, `applyTemplate`), `allow_fallbacks` on an error response (`false` keeps a mocked error final, otherwise the next fallback is tried, `gateway.tov` `chatOnce`), `GetStats` (`total_requests`, `mocked_requests`, `rule_hits`, `errors_generated`, `responses_generated`, per process, in the `/api/plugins` entry's `stats`), integer nanosecond durations for `latency` and `global_latency`, and the provider of a request that names none (the provider routing chose, `providerAndModel`). L159: it runs on the Anthropic Messages, GenAI generateContent, Bedrock Converse and InvokeModel and Cohere chat routes too (Bifrost's chat and Responses request types, which those routes become): a request a rule matches (or any request under default_behavior error or success) is served through chat and the mock answer comes back in the route's format (`MockerPlugin.appliesNative`, `nativehook.tov`); `message_regex` also reads the text parts of an array content. As in Bifrost it does not run on embeddings, rerank, count-tokens or batches. L174: it answers Responses requests too (/v1/responses and the routes served as it): the rules match the input's text (a string input, or the items' string content and text parts), and a success is answered as a Responses object, or for a streamed request as the one response.completed event Bifrost's mocker sends; errors keep their shape, and the latency applies (`plugins.tov` MockerPlugin.handlesOp, asResponses, inputText; `pluginroute.tov` hookedRun); e2e `l174_e2e`. |

L170: Responses streams are completed too (`response.output_text.delta` events, `JsonParserPlugin.postChunk`), and an invalid chunk is passed through instead of ending the stream; Bifrost runs the parser on chat and Responses streams only, so text completions are left as they are there too. L159: `strip_markdown_fences` also applies on the Anthropic Messages, GenAI, Bedrock and Cohere chat routes (they are served through chat while it is on, and the fences are taken out before the answer is converted). |

Covers convertparams, dropparams, azure_deepseek, text-to-chat, chat-to-responses, reasoning-only models and decisions (L82, L100). requestcopy.go is an internal clone, not a feature.
